You Feel Like Home

by Hazellury

Summary

Prince Caleb Whitmore has survived assassins, scandals, and the suffocating expectations of the Crown. He has never survived Arian Conti.

When an assassination attempt erupts at an art auction in Paris, a mysterious man in black gets Caleb out alive, only to disappear before Caleb can learn his name.

Four months later, the stranger returns as his new protector.

Arian is the youngest godfather of the Conti family, feared by the underworld and trusted by the Crown. He was sent to protect the prince.

Falling for him was never part of the plan.

Chapter 1: Prologue

Paris glitters differently at night. 

Beyond the tall windows of the museum, the city is a constellation of gold, somehow the roads are always rain slicked during this time of the year, street lamps burning against the darkness. 

Inside the light is softer. 

Hundreds of warm bulbs hang from the museum’s impossibly high ceilings, golden light in soft warm hue spills across the polished marble floors reflecting in the glass protecting centuries old masterpieces. Murals stretched across the walls, portraits hang from gilded frames and sculptures placed beneath the pools of amber light while champagne moves through the room in crystal flutes. 

Everything looks beautiful and expensive. 

Arian stands where the light doesn’t quite reach. 

His back rests against one of the enormous stone pillars bordering the main gallery, one ankle lazily crossed over the other. A black baseball cap sits low over his forehead, its shadow concealing most of his face. A dark leather jacket covers the rest of him, seeming deliberately out of place among the tailored tuxedos and designer suits. 

He looks like he doesn’t belong, and that is precisely the point. 

His gaze moves slowly across the room.

Security first, entrances, exits, balconies, windows blind spots, then the crowd. 

Arian is trained to notice everything without appearing to notice anything at all. 

For example the woman  in an emerald dress who keeps checking her phone. 

A man in a crisp tailored suit stands too close to the eastern entrance. 

Two members of the french security positioned near the private gallery. Three British guards near the prince. 

A camera crew moving through the crowd capturing everything. Nothing out of the ordinary.

Then his attention returns to the center of the room. 

To him. 

Prince Caleb Whitmore. 

The second son of the King. 

And if the whispers surrounding the royal family were to go by, the most troublesome member of it. 

Caleb is the reason half the people in the room even bothered to attend. 

The auction is organized under his patronage, with the proceeds going toward several charitable causes. It has received an absurd amount of attention for an event that was on paper, nothing more than an evening of art and philanthropy. 

But the royal family always understands the value of a spectacle. 

And Caleb understands it particularly well. 

There he stands, beneath the chandelier surrounded by cameras, diplomats, collectors and socialites, moving effortlessly from one conversation to the next. 

He smiles when spoken to, laughs when something amuses him, shakes hands, accepts compliments and poses for photographs.

He’s elegant and untouchable. Born into the kind of beauty that seems almost unfair. He looks exactly like what the world expects a prince to look like. 

Arian watches him for several seconds longer than necessary. 

This is the first time he’s seen him in person. For months, Caleb existed only as a name on briefing documents, photographs, security reports and intelligence files. 

Prince Caleb Whitmore, twenty-five years old, rightful heir according to the King’s wishes, despite the traditional line of succession being somewhat more complicated. 

Educated, charismatic and occasionally reckless. Several reports mentioned minor scandals of rebellious nature, his reputation for disappearing from royal functions whenever he grew bored and an affinity for art. 

He’s known for disregarding security protocols whenever he wants to, a handful of romantic entanglements that the British press finds endlessly entertaining. And a handful more that never reached the press. 

Arian has read everything. 

Apparently none of it prepared him for the actual man. Caleb moves again, and the crowd seems to move with him. Arian’s eyes narrow. 

The king has already warned him, that Caleb is going to be a problem. 

A soft burst of laughter reach him from across the gallery, Arian looks over. 

Caleb has stopped in front of one of the paintings, a woman next to him asks something and Caleb turns toward the painting, expression changing. 

“That one, its one of my favourites” his expression grows soft,  voice carries just enough for Arian to hear. 

“I think people misunderstand it, they see the darkness and assume its about about grief.” 

The crowd around him quiets instinctively. 

The painting depicts a woman sitting alone on the floor, a torn piece of paper rests in her hands, stained in blood red ink, and a single tear traces the side of her face. 

The woman next to Caleb smiles politely. 

“Isn’t it grief though?” She asks. 

Caleb gives a small smile and steps slightly closer. “I think it’s about wanting something you can’t have, or someone. It’s about loneliness, isn’t there something strangely beautiful about that kind of loneliness?” 

Arian stares at him, maybe its the way Caleb speaks about art, or maybe its simply that the prince has somehow managed to make an entire room disappear around himself. Arian has spent months studying Caleb Whitmore, and he never expected the prince to be so….interesting. 

And then Caleb is back to smiling widely again, more guarded, less open. 

“Maybe that’s why I like it, but we all have our own interpretation of art i suppose” he says, smiling beautifully at the woman. 

The crowd’s moving again with him to the next one, when Arian’s gaze shifts immediately. 

A movement near the eastern corridor catches his attention. One of his men, in a black suit emerges from the door. 

He stops beside Arian, without looking at him. 

“Boss, your route is clear.” 

Arian nods once. “The vehicle?” 

“Secure. The area behind the museum has been swept twice. Your men are in position.” 

“And the prince?” 

“Three guards on him at all times. More outside.” 

Arian glances toward Caleb, the man beside him lowers his voice. 

“If anything happens, you’re the contingency.” 

“I know.”

The man hands him a small black access card, Arian slips it inside his pocket. 

“Stay close to the eastern exit.” He murmurs. “Don’t let anyone see you with me.” 

The man nods and disappears into the crowd. Arian pushes himself away from the pillar and moves deeper into the gallery, keeping to the edges of the room.

The crowd thickens around Caleb as another group approaches him, Arian passes behind them. 

For the first time he sees Caleb up close, just a quick glance was enough to notice the details. 

A copper-brown vest fits neatly over a cream white shirt, the warm color almost glowing beneath the museum lights. His dark hair are soft and slightly disheveled, curls falling carelessly across his forehead. 

There was something almost unfair about his face. A kind of softness, that seems almost contradictory beside the confidence with which he carries himself. 

Then Caleb smiles and dimples appear. Arian looks away and continues toward the far end of the gallery. 

There are fewer people here, the majority of the guest are flocking around the prince. Arian stops before an enormous painting that covers almost an entire section of the wall. 

The painting is darker than others, a rainy street disappearing into fog, blurred neon lights reflect across the wet pavement, red green and purple bleeding together beneath the sky of black and charcoal gray. 

There is no person in the painting, yet it feels occupied with something that can only be felt. 

Loneliness, danger, peace. 

There is something unsettling about the quiet, Arian likes it. 

He doesn’t know how long he’s been standing there staring at the painting when suddenly he’s broken out of his reverie by the sound of soft footsteps approaching behind him. 

Arian doesn’t move, even after the footsteps stop beside him, he already knows who it is. 

“It’s a gorgeous one, isn’t it?” 

Caleb. 

Arian’s jaw tightens almost imperceptibly but he continues to look at the painting. 

Caleb stands beside him, leaving enough distance to be polite. For a few seconds, neither speaks. 

But then Caleb smiles. 

“I couldn’t help notice you earlier.” 

Arian’s gaze remains fixed ahead. 

“You’ve been standing here for quite some time.” 

Silence. 

Caleb glances sideways at him. “You don’t talk much, do you?” 

Arian finally looks toward him, only briefly and the prince is smiling, not the dazzling kind, he’s reserved for the cameras. A softer one, as if he genuinely wants to know the man standing beside him. 

Before Arian catches himself again for staring too long, he looks away, back at the painting. 

“I’m just looking.” 

A flat, curt response, hoping it’ll make the prince go away. But unexpectedly it makes him smile wider. 

“Right.” 

Arian turns away first, and takes one step to leave when the words follow him. 

“Wait.” The prince calls. 

Arian stops, but doesn’t turn around. Behind him Caleb seems about to say something else when another voice interrupts. 

“Your Highness.” 

Arian glances over his shoulder, one of the royal officers runs up to him. 

“Please stay with the group” 

Caleb sighs, “I am literally standing right here Paul, don’t get so paranoid on me.” 

Paul gives him an exhausted look of a man who has clearly heard this sentence several hundred times. 

“I’m doing my job sir.” 

“And I’m appreciating art.”

”You can appreciate it, where I can see you.” 

Caleb rolls his eyes, Arian watches the little exchange for half a second longer and then finds his cue to leave. 

He turns the left corner and almost reaches the eastern corridor when…

BOOM.

The sound tears through the walls of the museum. The floor beneath Arian’s boots tremble as his legs freeze for a millisecond. The glass shatters somewhere behind him, followed by a second explosion and screams erupt across the gallery. 

Arian turns on his heels instantly as the lights flicker violently overhead, for one brief second, the entire museum goes black. Then the emergency lights come alive in a wash of red and Arian’s hand goes beneath his jacket, fingers closing around the weapon hidden there. 

His eyes immediately finds the one person in the room who matters. 

Caleb. 

But the prince is nowhere in sight. 

The first gunshot cracks through the gallery, then another and the museum erupts. 

People scream and scatter in every direction, champagne glasses shattering against marble as people rush toward the exit frantically. Somewhere behind him, a woman is crying, security shout out orders over one another as more gunfire answers from the opposite end of the hall. 

Arian barely hears anything, eyes sharp and laser focused, searching. 

Caleb. 

He finds the British security near the eastern doors, weapons drawn and formation broken as they return fire toward the far end of the gallery. 

Arian’s jaw tightens, panic settling in hoping one of the guards got Caleb out already. They have protocols for this and have rehearsed this scenario many times. 

He moves through the crowd fast, slipping between panicked guests, hand remaining beneath the jacket, wrapped around the grip of his gun as his eyes sweep across the room. 

Just as he rounds a corner, he comes to a stop. 

There, near the windows. The prince is alive and apparently has decided that the middle of an active gunfire is the perfect time to become a fucking hero. 

Caleb is helping an elderly woman toward the nearest exit, one hand steadying her elbow while the other gestures sharply toward the doors. 

Another wave of people surge past him, and Caleb helps direct them away from the gunfire and toward the exit. Arian stares at him for one incredulous second. 

This idiot. 

He’s standing in the open, making himself visible and an easy target. 

Arian crosses the distance between them before he can think better of it, his hand close around Caleb’s wrist. 

Caleb barely has time to turn before Arian pulls him sharply backward. 

“What….”

Arian drags him behind the stone wall separating the gallery from the adjoining corridor. Caleb stumbles, as his back meets the wall, his eyes widen and for a second neither moves. 

Arian’s hand remains on his wrist, while his other hand holds the weapon low against his thigh, he quickly looks over the prince, checking for injuries, 

“Are you out of your mind” 

Caleb’s startled voice cuts through his thoughts. 

“What exactly do you think you’re….”

”Quiet.” 

Arian tilts his head, letting the shadow of his cap shift just enough for Caleb to see his eyes. 

“Come with me.” not a request and Caleb stares like he’s seen an alien. Perhaps he’s never been spoken to like this in his life. 

“Excuse me?”

Arian doesn’t bother answering, he tightens his grip and pulls him toward the corridor. 

“Hey, let go of me.” 

“Keep walking” 

“You cannot simply….”

“Keep your voice down”

”How dare you, I’m perfectly capable of…”

Another gunshot explodes somewhere behind them. Caleb flinches, and for a moment forgets that he’s being very forcefully dragged away by Arian. 

Arian takes it as his cue and pushes them through a narrow door concealed behind a wall, a dark staircase appears and Arian brings Caleb to the front, shielding him with his body. 

“Move.” 

Caleb stares at him as though considering whether to argue again, but then another burst of gunfire echoes through the museum and his expression changes, he simply nods and climbs the stairs, Arian behind him. 

They reach the rooftop doors, and Arian quickly shoves it open, and pulls Caleb through and slams the door shut behind them. 

The cold air rushes over them instantly. 

And then there’s silence. For a brief moment, there is nothing but the sound of their breathing. Caleb is standing directly in front of him, too close, back pressed into the door. 

His chest rise and fall rapidly from the climb, his hair once carefully styled for the camera have now fallen loose across his forehead, his clothes slightly rumpled. 

The moment breaks, when Caleb shoves him hard. 

“Are you completely out of your mind?” 

Arian lets himself be shoved, as the force moves him half a step back. 

Caleb comes closer. “You dragged me away from my security, forced me through a crowd at gunpoint and brought me onto a rooftop in the middle of an assassination attempt.”

Arian says nothing, just lets the prince throw a fit. 

Caleb’s eyes narrow. “Do you have any idea who I am?”

”Yes.”

 “Then you should understand precisely how absurd this is.”

”You’re alive.” 

“Should I thank you for stating the obvious?” 

Arian sighs, already regretting this, yes the king had warned him that the prince will be difficult, but he didn’t mention he’ll be downright insufferable. 

“You cannot simply abduct a member of the royal family, I can have you arrested and sentenced….” 

Arian catches his shoulder when Caleb moves too close and swiftly presses him back against the rooftop door. 

“Calm down, Your Highness.”

And Caleb goes quiet. 

“Unless you’d prefer to give whoever is trying to kill you a clear shot.” And that shuts the prince up.       

For approximately two seconds. 

“That would be considerably easier if you stop pointing a gun at me.” 

Feeling called out, Arian lowers the gun slightly. 

“You’re welcome.”

Caleb raises an eyebrow. “For what?”

”Saving your life.”

Caleb stares, and then his eyes narrow. 

“You caused this, didn’t you?”

Arian blinks, “What?” 

“Tch, you appear out of nowhere, drag me away and now you’re standing here behaving like I ought to thank you.”

”I didn’t cause the attack.”

”You certainly have the look of a man who might.” 

Arian inhales a breath trying to keep his temper under control and not lose it in front of the prince. 

“Stay here.” 

“I don’t take orders from strangers.”

”You do tonight.”

”I most certainly do not.” 

Another gunshot sounds from below, this one is closer to the staircase. 

Arian sees as Caleb freezes, the color draining slightly from his face. Arian steps closer immediately. 

“Quiet.” 

This time Caleb obeys. 

Footsteps echo somewhere beneath them, slow and heavy, someone is coming up the staircase. Arian positions himself between Caleb and the door, one hand ready at his weapon. 

When another gunshot fires, Caleb flinches behind him, as his fingers suddenly close around Arian’s arm. Arian can feel the prince is afraid, instinctively he holds onto the prince’s wrist and squeezes a little to keep him grounded. 

“Stay behind me.” 

Caleb nods, for once there is no argument. They wait, gun drawn out pointing at the door. Just then another shot is heard, and then silence. 

They wait for five seconds, ten, fifteen, nothing. 

His men have probably intercepted whoever made it this far. Eventually the footsteps disappear and Arian remains still for another few seconds, before finally releasing the breath he’s been holding. 

His grip on Caleb’s wrist loosens, and then he lets go. 

Caleb inhales sharply and leans back against the door, breathing heavily. 

Arian glances at him, his hair is a mess now, cheeks flush from the cold and adrenaline, he looks shaken but is trying hard to keep a calm exterior. Then he lifts his eyes and looks at Arian, carefully, as though trying to figure him out. 

“Who are you?”

Arian looks at him. “That’s not important.”

“It feels rather important at the moment.” 

“Are you hurt?” Arian changes the subject. Caleb glances down at himself. 

“No.” He looks back up. “You just saved my life.” 

Arian takes a deep breath and then extends his hand out to Caleb. 

“I need to get you out of here.”

Caleb stares at the offered hand and then at Arian. 

“I don’t think I should trust you.” 

Arian follows his gaze and understands, with a quiet sigh he slips the gun back beneath his jacket. 

Then he steps closer. “Whether you trust me or not, I’m your best chance of leaving this building alive.” 

“Is that meant to reassure me?” 

The prince is so damn stubborn, Arian wants to punch a wall but he keeps calm. “It’s meant to be true.”

That earns him a laugh, light and soft. The prince has nearly been killed twice in the last five minutes and somehow he’s laughing. 

“How exactly do you intend to save me while also threatening me in the same breath?” 

“You can start by not standing here like a fool and expose yourself to the very eager assassin currently on the loose looking for you.” 

Caleb’s smile is soft, completely inappropriate for the situation. 

Arian has a sudden unwelcome thought that perhaps the photographers haven’t done him justice. He immediately dismisses it. 

“You are one incredibly rude person.” 

“Come.” He doesn’t wait, simply takes Caleb’s arm and this time the prince doesn’t resist. They cross the rooftop toward the opposite wall, and stop beside a narrow door. 

“This leads to the rear staircase, the route is clear, your guards are waiting below, a car will take you back to the palace”

Caleb looks into the dark staircase and frowns. “You expect me to walk down there?” 

“Yes.” 

Caleb stares at the staircase again. “Right, so that’s not happening.”

”That is your only way out.” 

Caleb sighs. “I have apparently been rescued by a man with absolutely no appreciation for how reassuring he is.” 

Arian shoves the door open. “Go.” 

Caleb doesn’t move, still contemplating, instead he looks at him. “You really aren’t going to tell me your name?”

“No.” 

If Arian’s patience is beginning to fray, he doesn’t show. 

“Not even after all this?” Caleb waves his hand between the two gesturing at him and Arian. 

“No.” Arian says again. 

Caleb takes another sigh 

“You are terribly rude.” 

“Your Highness, there is an armed assassin somewhere in this museum who would very much like to kill you.” 

He gestures toward the staircase. 

“Your choices are remarkably simple. You can walk down those stairs and leave safely, or you can return to the gallery and wait for the man trying to kill you to find you.” 

“Seriously….”

”If you want to die that badly, I won’t stop you.” Arien says finally. 

At this point Arian expects another argument, instead, Caleb slowly pulls his arm out of Arian’s grip. 

Then he looks at him, really looks, as amusement fades from his face. Arian thinks perhaps he’s mistaken but a softer expression takes over on Caleb’s face. 

“Do I at least get a name?” He asks once again. 

Arian stays silent and Caleb sighs. 

“Well it was worth a shot.” 

Then he lifts his hand, and before Arian could understand what’s happening, the prince’s fingers catch the brim of his baseball cap. 

Arian inhales softly. “Don’t…”

But it’s too late, Caleb lifts the cap from his head, and for the first time there is nothing between them. 

Arian sees it happen as Caleb’s breath is caught, eyes wide slightly as they travel over Arian’s face, as though he expected something entirely different. A few strands of hair fall on Arian’s face from the wind and for a moment neither of them speak. 

“Wow.” Caleb’s voice drop as Arian’s jaw tightens. “You really are my knight in shining armour aren’t you?” 

Arian knows he shouldn’t stare too much, shouldn’t react the way his eyes follow the prince’s soft smile, should take the cap back that is still clutched in between Caleb’s fingers. 

Instead he just remains there, watching. Caleb’s eyes are impossibly bright beneath the night sky, and far too close. 

Arian reaches past him and shoves the door behind Caleb. 

“Leave.” 

His voice should have come out sharper, deeper, but he could only manage a soft breath, eyes still on Caleb’s face. 

Caleb blinks, the wind sweeps his soft hair across his forehead. “So that’s it?” 

“Yes.” 

“After saving my life?” 

“Go.” Arian realizes he’s almost on the verge of pleading at this point because he wants this to be over now. 

Caleb smiles, and then, apparently survival has taught him absolutely nothing, he tilts his head. 

“Not even a goodbye kiss?” 

Arian goes completely still. 

Of all the things the prince could have said. 

His gaze betrays him, before he can stop it, down to Caleb’s mouth. Soft and slightly red from the cold. Close enough to make a very stupid thought cross his mind. 

The prince notices, and something changes in his expression, the teasing softens as his gaze drops to Arian’s lips as well, and for one suspended second neither of them move. 

The world below starts to feel far away, they are so close that Arian can feel Caleb’s breath, could hear his own. One movement, one mistake and the line between duty and something far more dangerous will disappear. 

Arian inhales a sharp breath and steps back.

“Go.”

Caleb looks almost disappointed, and before he could say anything, Arian grabs his arm and pushes him out through the door, Caleb’s eyes widen. 

“Wait…” 

Arian closes the door shut and stands there for several seconds. He expects the prince to shout, to call him out but is only met with silence. 

Then footsteps start descending the staircase, one flight then another. 

Only when he’s certain Caleb has continued his path down the stairs, does Arian turn back toward the edge of the ledge. 

He waits, three minutes, five minutes, six. Then one of the lights move. 

Arian narrows his eyes as Caleb emerges from the rear entrance and 5 guards immediately surround him. 

Safe at last. 

Arian’s shoulders finally loosen, as he watches the prince being ushered toward the waiting vehicle. 

Then Caleb stops. 

Arian’s breath catch in his throat as the prince turns, and slowly almost instinctively looks upward, straight toward the rooftop, at him. 

For several seconds they simply stare at one another. Caleb doesn’t smile, neither does Arian. 

Arian looks away for a couple of seconds, and when he looks back again  Caleb is already inside the car. 

As the vehicle safely pulls away from the street, Arian allows himself to breathe. 

And then his expression hardens. The prince is safe, the job is done, now there is an assassin to find so Arian can put a bullet through his skull. 

He storms toward the door, taking out his gun, eyes fixed on the entrance of the museum below. 

Someone came dangerously close to kill Prince Caleb Whitmore tonight, and whoever did it, made a very serious mistake, they had threatened someone under his protection, and Arian intends to make the bastard regret it. 

His fingers tightens around his gun as he turns toward the staircase, the softness of the rooftop disappearing behind the cold certainty settling back into his bones.

He takes two steps toward the stairs before something feels wrong.

Arian stops.

His hand goes automatically to the top of his head.

His eyes closed.

The prince took his hat with him.

Of course he had.

Arian exhales slowly through his nose.

Shit.

Somehow, he has a feeling that losing his hat tonight is going to be the least of his problems.

Chapter 2

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

The newspaper lands in front of Caleb with enough force to make the silverware tremble. He glances down at it, the photograph splashed across the front page is unfortunately, a rather unflattering one. 

It’s him in the picture, standing in the middle of the crowded museum hall, one hand extended toward the elderly woman while people hurry around him in panic. His copper vest is unmistakable beneath the flashing cameras, his expression far less composed than the newspapers have apparently decided to make it seem. 

The headline is actually worse. 

CROWN PRINCE ACCUSED OF RECKLESS BEHAVIOR DURING PARIS ATTACK.

Caleb sighs. 

“Well,” he murmurs, reaching for his tea. “At least, they caught my good side.” 

His father standing at the head of the table sighs, expression stern. 

“Caleb.” 

There are few words in the English language capable of sounding quite as much like a death sentence when spoken by the King. 

Caleb takes a leisurely sip. 

“Yes, Father?” He says innocently. 

“You were specifically instructed to remain with your security.” 

“I did.”

”You disappeared and left them in the middle of an active assassination attempt.” 

“So I just relocated a bit, you are making it sound so dramatic.” 

Across the table, Eleanor snorts quietly into her coffee mug. Matilda sitting across from him hides the smallest smile behind her hand. 

Meanwhile Andrew remains emotionless, no expression on his face whatsoever like always. 

The eldest Whitmore sibling is sitting next to his father with the sort of immaculate composure that seems almost unnatural. Tall and broad shouldered, with perfectly styled blonde hair and striking green eyes. Andrew possesses the sort of face that belongs on the cover of a magazine dedicated to beautiful people with terrible intentions. 

Nevertheless, he looks every inch the future king. Caleb always thought that is rather unfortunate. 

“Caleb,  you were photographed helping civilians instead of taking cover.” The King reminds him firmly. 

“There was an elderly woman trapped near the window, I wanted to help.” 

“You should have let the security assist her.’ 

“They were busy trying to prevent everyone else from being shot.” Caleb reasons once again. 

“Caleb.” Another warning. 

“She was old.”  Caleb stares back. “She needed help, so I helped  her.” 

“You could have been killed.” 

“But I wasn’t.” 

“That is not the point Caleb.” The King says. 

“It seems to be the rather important part from where I’m sitting.” Caleb says. He doesn’t wanna argue anymore but he can’t seem to keep quiet, his father never understands. 

And then Andrew finally speaks. 

“You always have to make everything about yourself.” 

Caleb’s eyes move toward him. Andrew’s voice is calm, almost bored. “You see a camera and suddenly you have to perform.” Caleb raises his eyebrow. 

“I wasn’t aware helping an elderly woman constituted a performance.” 

Andrew’s face hardens. “Not everything requires an audience, you have no idea what kind of responsibility your behavior creates for the rest of us. You cannot even take accountability for it, how will you take the crown when it’s your time?” 

Caleb leans back in his chair, there it is again, the speech that Andrew has been giving him for years. 

“You know,” Caleb says lightly, “I don’t actually want the crown, and you don’t have to keep reminding me.” 

“I’ll remind you whenever necessary, questioning your duty is not what is expected of a prince.” 

Caleb’s smile fades slightly. What does Andrew know about duty when all his tactics have been to manipulate father into taking the crown. 

“I think you’ve made your position quite clear, Andrew, I’d appreciate you saving the lecture for the next time.” 

Andrew rises from his chair. The movement is so sudden it startles Caleb. And then he sees as his big brother walks around the table and stops in front of him. Caleb slightly stiffens. 

“You are reckless.” Andrew says quietly. “And every time you make a mistake, someone else has to clean it up.” 

Andrew bends slightly, bringing himself closer to Caleb’s face. One of the things that Andrew seems to do a lot is invade his personal space, to the point it sometimes terrifies Caleb and leaves an unsettling feeling in his stomach. 

“If you don’t start taking these threats seriously, one day, you’re going to end up dead in a ditch.” 

His hand comes up and his fingers close around Caleb’s chin, titling his face up, not hard enough to hurt, but enough to make a point. 

Caleb instinctively leans backward, something cold crawls beneath his skin. 

Andrew smiles. “People talk, you may not care but I do, especially when it's about you.”

For a second, neither move, Caleb sits still until Andrew releases him and steps back. Caleb lets out a breath he doesn’t know he is holding. 

“Alright, Andrew, you’ve made your point” Matilda finally speaks, her voice calm yet firm. Her eyes move to Caleb. 

Caleb hopes Matilda doesn’t notice his discomfort. He absolutely hates that he allows Andrew to have so much control over him. It makes his skin crawl, whenever his older brother gets like this. 

Matilda is the eldest daughter of the Whitmore siblings, and there is something about her that makes people instinctively listen when she speaks. Long black hair falling over her shoulders, framing features so similar to Caleb’s that the resemblance is unmistakable. But where Caleb’s presence is charming and playful, Matilda is always composed. 

She carries herself like someone who has spent her entire life learning how to survive rooms full of people who underestimate her. Had circumstances been different, she would be the next in line for the Crown. 

Instead, centuries of tradition decided that being born a woman makes her somehow less suited to rule. 

Matilda has never forgiven the institution for that. Nor has she allowed it to stop her. 

The King trusts her more than almost anyone in the palace, she is his strategist when political matters become too complicated for simple diplomacy. 

Andrew handles alliances but Matilda understands the monarchy from inside, out. And Caleb loves her for it. 

“Caleb,” she says gently. “How did you get out?”

”What?”

”The museum. The security informed us that you were able to reach them on time, did you find the exit yourself?” 

For a moment Caleb says nothing. 

And then Paris returns to him, not the gunshots or the screaming, him. The black baseball cap, the leather jacket, a hand firmly closing around his wrist, and a deep voice telling him…

Come with me

Caleb’s fingers tighten unconsciously around his teacup. The thing is he hasn’t been able to stop his brain from thinking about the man who saved him that night. He could still faintly remember the scent, something warm and woody beneath a trace of smoke. He could still remember the way those dark eyes watched him, as though Caleb was simply….Caleb. 

Nothing more, nothing less. 

And then there was that hat, the one that he had taken it with him, technically stole it, now currently hidden underneath the drawer in his bedroom. Not exactly his proudest moment, but when he was making his way down that dark staircase alone, every nerve in his body was screaming. He kept his fingers wrapped around the brim of the cap, allowing to feel it, to keep him grounded. The hat was the proof that the man had been real, and not someone he imagined. 

“Caleb?’ 

Matilda’s voice pulls him back, he blinks and then quickly recovers, smiling. 

“Sorry, I… uh escaped through the roof.”

Matilda briefly glances at her father, before she looks back at Caleb again. “How’d you get to the roof?” 

Caleb feels a bit cornered, because Matilda is asking too many questions now. 

“Uhh.. you know..I guess I was lucky enough to slip away and found the exit on time.” 

He doesn't meet Matilda’s eyes and she doesn’t look convinced but lets it go. Eleanor next to Matilda chuckles. 

“You had quite an adventurous evening little brother, I’m kind of jealous.” She says playfully, lightning the mood and Caleb laughs with her. 

“More like a suicidal evening” Andrew mutters. Eleanor rolls her eyes. 

Matilda lets out a small sigh and smiles at Caleb. “For what it’s worth, the assassin’s dead.” She says. 

Caleb’s head snaps toward her, “Wait… they got him?” 

“One of the guards found him before he could escape, according to the reports, he was roaming the museum searching for you, when the guard got to him and shot him on sight.” 

“Do we know which security personnel did it?” Caleb asks, he doesn't know why he asked that, perhaps he’s just hoping against hope that he can find something about him. 

Caleb notices Matilda shares another look with her father before looking back at Caleb.

“We…have yet to find out, I’ll be having a meeting with the security team tomorrow morning and get the briefings of the incident.” 

Caleb knows she’s bullshitting, the news doesn’t bring the relief he expected, because whoever that man on the rooftop was, he seemed terrifyingly certain that the assassin wouldn’t reach him. 

And it still doesn’t answer Caleb’s question, as to why he had been there, where had he come from and why the memory of him, his lingering touch and his voice had stayed with him. 

The King finally exhales and gets up from his seat. He’s been silent throughout the exchange, watching his children with an expression Caleb can’t quite decipher. 

His father is often like this, quietest when he knows something. Caleb learned a long time ago, the silence from the king is rarely comforting. 

“We are glad you are safe son, just please don’t do something foolish like this again.” His tone comes out soft and gentle. 

Caleb gives him a small smile. “Thank you.” 

“I am serious Caleb, this cannot happen again.” 

“I’ll do my best.” 

“No, you will do better. One more reckless decision and I will have to take drastic measures.” His father’s voice hardens. Caleb remains smiling. 

“Understood.” 

He can feel Andrew watching him, like always, Caleb refuses to look. 

“Matilda, come with me. I need your opinion on the reports from Brussels.” 

Matilda pushes her chair back and nods curtly. Before leaving she turns toward Caleb. Caleb looks at her and sees the soft smile on her face, comforting and reassuring. 

“I’m glad you’re alive, little one.” She says. Caleb returns her smiles. 

“I am not going anywhere.” 

Matilda squeezes his shoulder. “See that you don’t.” Then she follows their father out. 

Eleanor waits approximately three seconds before standing up, she comes around the table and bumps her shoulder against Caleb. 

“Sooo, rifle shooting?” 

Caleb  glances at her, a grin appearing on his face already. “You know I’ll never say no to that.” 

“Good, let's get out of this dull place.” She pulls him up and hooks her arm through his. 

Caleb happily lets her drag him. As they begin walking toward the garden, he feels it. 

Andrew’s gaze follows him, watching, but he doesn't turn around. Andrew can sit in one place for hours if he wants to, and apparently staring at Caleb is an excellent way to spend an afternoon. 

Weirdo. 

He tightens his arm around Eleanor as they leave, and for once he wants nothing to do with the crown or assassination attempts and certainly nothing to do with his brother. 

Some rifle shooting ought to quiet his mind for a while. But even then, inconveniently, there’s one thing he still can’t seem to stop thinking about. 

The stranger in the black leather jacket beneath his trembling fingers. 

Pair of dark eyes, and deep voice telling him to listen.

And the black hat hidden in the drawer, in his bedroom. 

++++

The rifle cracks across the grounds, Caleb lowers the weapon and watches the target swing gently in the distance. 

“Bullseye.” 

Eleanor glances sideways and snorts. “Show-off.”

“And you say that as though it's a bad thing” 

“It is when you’re so insufferable about it.” Eleanor retorts, she’s lifting up her rifle and aiming at the target, when a voice comes from behind. 

“Big brother.” 

Caleb barely has time to turn before a blur  of dark hair collides with his side. He laughs, catching Cecily around the shoulders as she wraps both arms around him. 

“Good heavens, where do you get all that strength in that little body of yours.” 

“I heard you nearly got yourself killed.” 

“Nearly?” Caleb chuckles. Cecily pulls back, narrowing her eyes. “I mean I survived so it’s all good.” 

“Actually, I heard all about how you managed to escape through the rooftop by yourself, that must have felt like a movie”

“Tch, that’s what I said.” Eleanor intervenes from beside them, eyes still locked to her target. 

“Are you sure you’re alright?” Cecily asks, looking at his face as if inspecting to find any injuries. 

“I am perfectly fine.” Caleb reassures her, ruffling her hair as she tries to swat his hand away. 

Cecily being the youngest of the Whitmore's has been spoiled accordingly, and everyone knows it. She’s charming enough to get away with most of the things and clever enough to know exactly when to use that charm. 

Although Caleb gets along well with all his sisters, he’s especially been powerless against Cecily.

“I’ve been studying all morning. I have three chapters of constitutional history to finish and a tutor who apparently  believes that sleep is an optional human function.” 

“That sounds dreadful.” 

“It is dreadful, which is exactly why I have decided to drop out.” 

“You’ve said that every week for the past six months.” Eleanor says, eyes laser focused on the target, as she aims with her rifle. 

“And one day I shall mean it.” 

Caleb smiles at his sisters. 

For a while everything feels normal. No Assassins, or newspaper headlines, or suffocating dinners. Just his sisters and the familiar rhythm of their teasing. 

“Caleb, what actually happened in Paris?” Cecily asks, expression changing. That’s the thing about her, she is always perceptive and notices things. 

Caleb lets out a sigh, and fakes a smile. “I’ve already told everyone.” 

“You said you got lucky, and that usually means there’s a version of a story you're not telling anyone.” 

“Well that's the only version you’ll get out of me because it's the truth.” 

“Hmm, you’re hiding something.” 

“I am not, and you are annoyingly persistent.” 

“I am the youngest, being annoying is my birthright.” 

Eleanor laughs, as she joins them. “Cecily just drop it, I think it’s best we put this whole assassination shit show behind us.” 

“Ugh fine….I’ll drop it, at least for now.” 

“I think after everything, we deserve a celebration.” Eleanor suggests.

“Honestly I could use a drink, a strong one.” Cecily says.

“That’s your solution to everything.” Eleanor retorts, rolling her eyes. 

“I nearly lost my brother to an assassin, I deserve a champagne.” 

“Okay, I am afraid I have to support her logic.” Caleb agrees. 

Eleanor sighs. “Fine, let’s go before the two of you become unbearable.” 

++++

The lounge occupies an entire wing of the palace, tucked away from formal halls and prying eyes. Low lighting washes over deep velvet sofas and a polished marble bar stands against one wall, lined with crystal glasses and an impressive collection of drinks. 

In the center, an open space serves as a dance floor, making the room feel less like part of the palace and more like a private club.  

The royal family’s lounge is the closest thing any of them have to a normal night out. Caleb of all people can’t exactly walk into a London club without causing a minor diplomatic incident. 

So they built their own version of one. A place where the siblings disappear when they want to forget they are royal for a few hours. 

By the time Caleb arrives, music is already playing, while champagne bottles are popping and people are already on the dance floor. Looks like Cecily has invited half a dozen friends.

Caleb loosens the collar of his white dress shirt, finally feeling like he can breathe. “Much better.” 

Eleanor hands him a glass. “To surviving Paris.”

Caleb raises his glass. “To surviving this family.” 

They clink glasses together, as Cecily appears beside him, wrapping her arms around his.

“There’s someone else coming.” 

Caleb looks at her and frowns, Cecily’s smile widens. 

“Good lord Cecily, what did you do now?” 

“Don’t be dramatic, I am doing you a favor.” 

“You….” Before he can answer, a familiar voice comes from behind him. 

“Hello, Your Highness.” 

Oh…of course. 

Caleb turns. And there he is, tall, dark haired, unfairly handsome. 

Landon Reed is the son of a senior parliamentary figure, who grew up around politics without ever seeming particularly interested in inheriting it. 

Caleb chuckles half heartedly, as the man walks up to him. “You’re kidding me right?” 

He’s dressed in a loose black shirt, with the sleeves rolled carelessly to his forearms, hair styled with enough effort to look effortless. 

He is charming, smart, and kind,  and unfortunately for Caleb, very good at making him forget himself in the bedroom. They have never been serious, and always had a non exclusive agreement. Until a month ago, when Landon admitted that he had started wanting something more. 

Caleb couldn’t give him that, and things just ended. At least for a while. 

But now he’s here again, and he’s smiling, that oh so charming smile. 

“It’s been a while, Caleb.” His voice is always a little bit softer when he speaks to him. 

Caleb swallows. 

“It has.”

Landon steps closer and takes his hand, turning it gently, he presses a kiss on his knuckles. Caleb’s breath catches in his throat. 

“Good to see you alive and well, my prince.”

Caleb retrieves his hand almost instantly. “Don’t.” Landon’s smile softens. 

“Don’t what?” 

“Landon, why are you here?”

“It’s been a month, I hoped you at least missed me?” 

Caleb rolls his eyes. “Tch. I was rather busy surviving psycho killer on the loose. Forgive me for not finding the time to miss your insufferably arrogant face” 

Landon throws his head back, laughter bubbling out of him. “And you’re still impossible.” 

“I prefer charming.” 

Landon smirks, and steps a bit closer, Caleb doesn’t move, just lets him get into his space. “That too.” Landon whispers. 

Across the room Eleanor and Cecily exchange delighted looks, and Caleb glares at them. 

As the evening dissolves into laughter, and the music grows louder, Caleb manages to forget everything. Landon stays next to him at all times, though they just chat casually, catching up, until…

“I am glad you’re alive.” Landon says in a softer voice, Caleb smiles, holding onto his drink a little tighter. 

“I’m not that easy to get rid of.” 

“Hmm, to be honest, I’d rather not test that theory.” 

Caleb smiles, this time genuinely. 

“I wasn’t ready to lose you.” Landon says suddenly, seriously. Something about that makes Caleb’s chest tighten. “I just want you to know that Caleb.” Landon steps a little closer, their hands almost touching, resting on the bar counter. 

“Thank you.” Caleb replies softly. 

“You know I…I’ve been meaning to…” Landon starts and then lets out a breath as Caleb watches him patiently. “Just that, I don’t like how we left things off last time, what we had was good, wasn't it?” Landon asks, and Caleb doesn’t miss a hint of hope in his eyes. 

“Landon...”

”I know, I know what we talked about, but I don't regret what we had Caleb.” 

And for a brief moment Caleb sees the sincerity in his eyes and smiles softly. “Neither do I.”

Landon steps slightly closer, close enough that their breaths brush on each other's faces. “Then maybe we could try again?” 

Caleb’s heart sinks, he opens his mouth to speak but nothing comes out. 

Suddenly, flashes of a black baseball cap, and dark eyes come back to him, as a quiet voice whispers in his ear. 

You need to leave now. 

Caleb blinks as memories of that night vanish when he feels Landon’s hand gently squeezing his arm. 

“Caleb?”

“Sorry.” 

“You okay?” 

“Yeah, I’m fine.” He pushes himself slightly away from Landon creating some distance and takes a long sip of his drink. 

“Hey, I know you must be shaken after what happened at the museum, but I’m…”

”I said I’m fine.” 

Landon goes quiet, and pulls away just a little bit as well, understanding that Caleb needs some space. 

“Alright.” Landon’s voice is soft, he feels his hand still resting gently on Caleb’s arm and something stirs in his heart. Guilt perhaps? Landon has always been good, no rudeness or manipulation behind his actions, he genuinely cares. “Let me get you another drink.” 

He turns.

”Landon.” 

Landon suddenly stops and turns back to Caleb. Caleb hesitates for a second before he draws closer to him again, grabbing the front of his shirt’s collar. “Do you want to get out of here?” 

Landon blinks at him a couple times, Caleb waits as a slow smile appears on Landon's face. 

“Are you sure?” 

Caleb nods. “Yes.” 

And they slip away from the party. 

++++

Caleb doesn’t give himself time to reconsider, as he wraps his arms around Landon and kisses him. Landon responds immediately. They burst into his bedroom as he catches Landon’s hand and pulls him closer, toward the bed. 

For a few precious seconds, Caleb lets himself disappear into something familiar. 

No Crown, no assassination attempts or a mysterious savior telling him to listen. 

He focuses on the hands around his waist, Landon’s mouth on his neck and the reassuring familiarity of someone he’s known before. 

He kisses Landon harder almost desperately as they fall on the bed together. Caleb pulls him closer, his fingers fumbling impatiently with the buttons of Landon’s shirt. 

Landon laughs softly against his mouth. 

“Someone’s in a hurry.” 

“Don’t talk.”

Caleb kisses him again, coaxing his mouth open to feel his tongue, he doesn’t want to think, he doesn't want to remember the rooftop or the strange warmth that had settled over him when the man stood between him and the gunfire, protecting him. 

It’s been five days since the incident, five days since his return back to London, yet the stranger's voice stays in his head.

He wants something he understands right now, something that doesn't make his chest ache. 

Landon kisses along his jaw, and Caleb closes his eyes. 

Focus. 

But his mind betrays him. 

I need to get you out of here. 

He forces his eyes open and Landon is still here, familiar and beautiful. So why does his heart feel like it’s somewhere else?

“Caleb?” 

“I am fine….just, don’t stop.”

Landon smirks, and obliges, as he goes down to trail open mouthed kisses along his neck and Caleb closes his eyes again, focusing on the touch. 

Caleb barely notices, as Landon reaches his hand out and pulls the bedside drawer open, and his hand disappears searching for condoms when something catches Caleb’s eyes. 

The black baseball cap slips free and lands on the floor, Caleb freezes, every ounce of urgency disappears as his entire body goes still. 

Shit, he forgot he had put it there. 

Landon doesn’t notice, until his hand is back on Caleb again with a couple of condom packets.

“Stop.” Caleb gasps. 

Landon immediately pulls away. 

“Caleb?” 

Caleb stares down at the hat, still lying carelessly on the floor. 

His hat. 

The one he so quietly took with him, and carried it down the staircase when he was terrified. The one that smells faintly of sandalwood and smoke. 

Suddenly he can’t breathe. 

He sits upright and pushes Landon away. 

“Caleb…hey.”

”Wait.” Caleb’s voice cracks as he tries to catch his breath. 

Landon’s expression changes instantly, he quickly pulls away from Caleb to give him some space. 

“Caleb, take it easy, it’s okay.” Landon says gently and Caleb finally looks up at him. The man is looking at him all worried, and Caleb feels bad for him. 

Landon gently touches his cheek, and Caleb flinches. Not because Landon had hurt but because the touch felt wrong. 

It’s not his. 

The realization scares him more than anything else. He gently removes Landon’s hand. 

“I’m sorry.” 

Landon looks at him for a moment then nods. “It’s okay.” 

And somehow that understanding and kindness makes Caleb feel even worse. 

“I thought I could.” 

“You don’t have to apologize, it’s okay.” 

Caleb looks up at him, there is disappointment in his eyes, but no anger or resentment. Landon gets up from the bed and reaches for his shirt to pull it back on. 

“Let’s go back to the party.” 

“You’re not upset?” Caleb asks. 

Landon gives him a small smile. “Of course I’m disappointed, but I am not going to make you feel guilty for it.” 

“Landon….”

“I’ll see you downstairs.” 

At the door, Landon pauses and turns back to Caleb. “Take your time.” And then he’s gone. 

The room becomes quiet and Caleb stares at his hands, his eyes go back to the hat on the floor, and he slowly reaches down to pick it up. 

For several seconds he doesn't move and then slowly presses it against his chest, closing his eyes. 

He doesn't understand why a stranger he’s met for perhaps ten minutes is living inside his head like this. He doesn't even know if he'll ever see him again. 

And yet the warmth of his hand around his wrist, the sound of his voice, the way his body stood between Caleb and the world, he can’t seem to forget any of it. 

Caleb doesn't know how long he’s been sitting here on his bed clutching onto the hat like a fool. 

“God, this is ridiculous.” 

He quickly shoves the hat back into the drawer. 

Then, after a moment of hesitation, he reaches in again and pushes it all the way back, and slams the drawer shut. 

Out of sight. 

Out of mind. 

At least that’s the idea. 

Caleb falls backwards onto his pillows, and covers his face with both hands. He’s survived assassination attempts, his father’s lecture and even Andrew’s tormenting. 

Yet somehow, none of it has managed to make him feel nearly as helpless as a man whose name he doesn't even know. 

He groans into his hands realizing he’s quite possibly, completely screwed. 

Chapter End Notes

Call him Caleb Yearning Whitmore at this point. :p

Hope you enjoyed this chapter!

Chapter 3: Chapter 3

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Caleb loves horses. 

There’s something about the way he swings himself on the saddle that makes the rest of the world fall away. All the expectations, the cameras and the endless conversations about diplomacy, successions and appearances seem to just disappear. 

There’s only his beautiful horse, the wind against his face, the rhythmic thunder of the hooves and the familiar weight of the polo mallet in his hand. 

Out here, Caleb isn’t the fourth child of the Whitmore Family. Or the royal prince, or the family’s favourite disappointment who always turns into a perpetual subject of newspaper speculation. 

He is simply Caleb. And he is very, very good at this. 

He leans forward, as his horse accelerates, black mane whipping violently as the ball shoots across the grass ahead of him. Caleb follows and swings fast. 

The crack of the mallet against the ball echoes across the field, and it flies through the goalposts. 

The crowd erupts. 

Caleb straightens in the saddle with a grin, lifting his mallet triumphantly as the cheers roll across the ground. From somewhere on the opposite side of the field comes a sharp whistle. 

Caleb turns his head to find Eleanor grinning at him.

“Show-off.” She yells as he laughs. 

“You’re only saying that because you haven't scored yet!”

Eleanor responds with an offended gesture before turning her horse back toward the play. Their younger sister, Cecily is considerably less restrained. 

“Go, Caleb!” She cheers from a distance as Caleb takes another score. 

This is what he loves about these days. For a few hours they are simply just siblings, not heirs or royals competing for the crown as the political pieces placed by their father.

The annual Polo and Equestrian Tournament has been a royal tradition for decades. Officially it's a sporting event, in practice, it's something much larger. 

Half of London’s elite seem to gather on the sprawling grounds wherever the royal family hosts it. Foreign royals occupy the private viewing boxes. Ministers, diplomats, industrialists and aristocrats fill the grandstands alongside celebrities and influential figures who have managed to secure invitations. 

The event is photographed from practically every angle, where every outfit is analysed, and every interaction becomes a gossip, and every member of the Whitmore family is expected to perform their part. 

And Caleb is so good at that, he smiles for the camera, waves when expected, and charms people he has no intention of remembering. 

Andrew, naturally, has declined to participate. He considers these tournaments beneath his time. According to him, spending an afternoon chasing a ball around a field is hardly an appropriate use of the future king’s schedule. 

Caleb personally thinks Andrew could use a little less scheduling and considerably more sunlight. 

Fortunately, his older brother isn’t even in London. He’s been sent abroad on a diplomatic assignment several days earlier, which means Caleb can enjoy at least one royal event without having Andrew’s cold stare follow him around. 

His horse suddenly shifts beneath him, Caleb adjusts to it easily and then he feels something coming toward him fast. 

Across the field, Matilda is coming straight toward him, and he recognizes the look on her face immediately. The smirk on her face is all too familiar, cause the woman is ready to best the entire crowd with one move. 

She accelerates and Caleb quickly does the same. 

For several seconds they ride shoulder to shoulder, each trying to reach the ball first, and then Caleb swings, and misses. 

Fuck.

Matilda sweeps in, her mallet connects cleanly, sending the ball sailing through the goal. 

The crowd cheers again, as Caleb stares in disbelief. 

“That was mine!” 

Matilda turns in the saddle laughing. “You were too slow.” 

“You literally stole it from under my horse.” 

“That sounds suspiciously like an excuse.” Matilda says, as she casually fixes her glove, still smirking, she raises the mallet in victory and cheers with the crowd. 

“Try to keep up, little one.” 

Caleb pouts as Matilda laughs and rides away. Caleb shakes his head, unable to stop the smile from returning. There are very few people in the world he would willingly lose to.

Matilda is one of them. 

The match continues, until the whistle finally sounds for the break. 

Caleb guides his horse toward the stables, breathing hard but grinning as he dismounts. The stable area is considerably quieter than the field. Servants and grooms move between horses, offering water and cooling them down while the players prepare for the next round. 

Caleb loosens his gloves and runs a hand through his damp hair, as Eleanor appears beside him almost immediately.

“You’re on fire today.” 

“Thank you.” 

She rolls her eyes. “That wasn't a compliment.” 

“And yet, I took it as one.” He winks at her. She laughs and gives him a solid thump between the shoulders, causing him to nearly stumble. 

Just then, Paul appears before Caleb, holding out a bottle of water. 

Paul has been assigned to him years ago, and has developed the unfortunate distinction of being the man responsible for preventing Caleb from doing anything catastrophically stupid. 

It’s a losing battle everyday. 

Caleb takes a long sip of the drink, before noticing the expression on his assistant’s face. 

“Don’t look at me like that.” 

Paul folds his arms, a crease forming across his forehead. “I haven’t said anything.” 

“You don’t have to. I know that face.” 

“And yet, Your Highness continues to give me reasons to make it.” 

Caleb smiles, as Paul lets out a small sigh. 

“Your Highness, I would like to remind you that last time you decided to perform one of your little stunts on horseback, I spent three hours explaining to your father why you thought jumping over a hedge was a perfectly reasonable idea.” 

“It was a very impressive hedge.” 

“It was six feet tall.” 

“And I cleared it with flying colors. See Paul, you worry over nothing.” 

“You nearly broke your neck.” Paul explains in a deadpanned expression. 

“Well I didn’t” 

Paul sighs again. “That is precisely the problem with you.” 

“Relax, I’ve been riding since I could walk.” Caleb says as he takes another sip from the bottle. 

“That is not what worries me.” Caleb glances at him as Paul takes the bottle back from Caleb, and brings up a towel to wipe it gently down his face. “You enjoy doing things that make me question my career choices, and age me faster.” 

Caleb laughs. “That’s a sweet confession Paul.” 

“Please…just behave.” 

“I am going to win this tournament and steal Matilda’s victory right back.” Caleb says as he wears his helmet again, and grabs his mallet. 

“I would prefer you remain alive.” 

“Have some faith in me” 

“It’s my blood pressure I’m worried about." he murmurs, causing Caleb to laugh again as he turns back to his horse. .  

“Alright, wish me luck” 

“Don’t die” Paul deadpans again. 

Caleb swings back into the saddle again, as the second half of the game is about to begin. He guides his horse back toward the field, the noise grows louder as they approach, applause and music playing in the background. 

He can already see the royal enclosure above the field. His father sits in the centre, surrounded by security, and a collection of important figures from across Europe. Caleb barely glances toward them. 

Until something catches his attention. 

A man is seated beside the King. Caleb slows down. 

He can't see him clearly. The stadium is enormous, and the royal enclosure is situated high above the field. 

Suddenly, Caleb can’t see anywhere else. There is a man sitting next to his father, in a black suit, and dark sunglasses. Everyone around him is talking, but the man isn’t. 

He’s sitting there composed, perfectly motionless, as if the noise of the entire event exists somewhere far beyond him. 

Something strange moves through Caleb’s chest, a feeling he can’t immediately place. 

Then the man turns his head slightly as his father says something to him, Caleb can’t see it clearly but a feeling of familiarity washes over him. The angle of his face, the sharp jawline even though his eyes are covered by sunglasses, it’s still so vaguely familiar.

It can’t be. 

Caleb’s heart stumbles. 

Four months. It’s been months since Paris, since the explosions, and the gunshots, and since a stranger in black appeared from nowhere and dragged him away from death. 

Caleb had stopped thinking about it, at least he had gotten better. 

And God he still tries not to remember. 

For the first few weeks, it was difficult, the black baseball cap had remained hidden beneath his pillow, he had told himself it was because it had been the only thing he had to hold onto during those terrifying minutes in the stairwell. 

Eventually he had moved it to the back of his drawer. 

He didn't think about the stranger every day anymore. At least that is what he keeps telling himself. But sometimes, late at night, Caleb still remembers. 

The woody, smoky scent of his jacket, and the warmth of his hand around Caleb’s wrist. As though Caleb is something worth protecting, as though, for those few minutes, nothing else in the world mattered. 

Caleb swallows, as the man nods slightly to whatever his father is saying to him. For one fleeting second, the sunlight catches the side of his face and Caleb’s pulse jumps. 

It can’t be him, it absolutely cannot be the stranger from Paris sitting beside his father. 

“Caleb!” 

Cecily’s voice snaps through his thoughts. He turns, she is pointing toward the field impatiently.  

“Are you planning to join us or shall we send someone to collect you?” 

Caleb blinks. 

Oh, right, the game. 

Caleb looks down at his horse, and then back toward the royal enclosure. The man is still there. 

He forces himself to look away as he nudges his horse and starts heading into the field.  

His heart is beating too loud in his ear and he can't shake the feeling that somehow, impossibly, he just looked directly at the ghost that had been haunting him for four months. 

Which is utterly ridiculous and Caleb convinces himself that his mind is simply just playing tricks on him. 

The whistle sounds and Caleb rides on. But for the first time, he isn’t thinking about the game. The remainder of the match, he keeps missing shots, he would normally have made without thinking. 

Matilda steals the ball from him twice. Eleanor scores while Caleb is looking entirely in the wrong direction. Even Cecily shouts at him from the other side to pay attention.  

He barely hears her, or anyone. His eyes keep wandering back to the enclosure, even though from this distance he can barely see anything, still his heart isn’t in the right place anymore. 

The match finally ends, and Caleb barely notices. 

The crowd erupts around him, but the first thing Caleb does is turn his horse back around toward the stands. His gaze immediately finds the place where the stranger had been sitting. 

Empty. 

Caleb’s stomach drops, he stares at the vacant seats, before looking everywhere else. And then there, at the stairs, there’s a figure disappearing through the upper passageway. 

Black suit, dark hair. 

There are two royal guards behind the man, walking several steps away from him. Caleb’s heart begins pounding and he doesn’t think. 

He simply drops the reins, dismounts and starts toward the stands. 

“Caleb!”

Someone calls his name, he ignores them. He pushes through the crowd, eyes fixed on the staircase, but by the time he reaches it, there is no one there. 

He goes up the staircase and through the door, but finds himself in an empty corridor. Caleb stands there, breathing harder than he should have been. He doesn't know exactly what he was expecting to find. 

How ridiculous and bizarre it would be to find a man in a baseball cap standing at the end of the corridor. 

Hey, you look like the guy who saved my life in Paris, are you him? 

He is definitely losing his mind because there is no one here. 

He looks back at the royal enclosure, but the empty chair stares back at him. 

Four months of trying not to think about the stranger, and convincing himself that whatever happened in Paris was nothing more than an extraordinary night that he will eventually forget. 

And now the memories have appeared again, as if they never really disappeared at all. 

Caleb goes back to the enclosure, and stares at the empty seat. Then very quietly he removes his helmet and runs his hand through his hair as if that would shake the thoughts out of his head. 

“What the hell is wrong with me”

++++

Back in his room, Caleb is alone in his balcony, one hand resting against the stone railing as he stares out at the London’s skyline while the sun has almost set. 

The city stretches endlessly beneath him, a sea of lights glittering against the dark. Usually, he loves this view, from this height everything looks smaller, but tonight, it does nothing for him. 

His mind is still back at the grounds where the crowd was loud and beside his father sat a distant figure dressed entirely in black. 

Caleb exhales slowly, watching the smoke curl from between his fingers. 

He rarely smokes, but tonight he feels like taking one, just to quiet the thoughts running through his head. 

Paul knows that, which is precisely why the soft knock at the door followed by a long, unimpressed sigh doesn’t faze him. 

“Your Highness.” 

He smiles faintly, “Paul.” 

A moment later footsteps approach across the marble floor. Caleb continues staring ahead, taking another drag from the cigarette. 

He barely has time to enjoy it before Paul reaches over, plucks it straight from his fingers and crushes it against the stone ledge. Caleb stares at him. 

“Did you really just do that?” 

“Yes.” 

“You realize I’m technically your employer.” 

“And you realize cigarettes are disgusting." 

Caleb rolls his eyes and turns back toward the city. 

“I am not in the mood for another lecture.” 

“Good. Because I’ve given you the same one at least twenty times and you’ve ignored it every single time.” 

A reluctant smile tugs at Caleb’s mouth. Paul stops next to him and folds his arm against the railing. For a few moments, neither of them speak, then Paul’s voice softens. 

“You left lunch early.” 

Caleb’s smile fades. “I was tired.” 

“You disappeared before dessert.” 

“Tragic, I know.” Caleb says, shrugging his shoulders. 

Paul studies him from the corner of his eye. “Are you alright?” 

Caleb doesn’t meet his eyes. He knows Paul can take one look at him and see something’s wrong. Paul is that one person in the palace who pretty much spends the most time with him, over the years they have developed a sort of friendship where they understand each other. Paul is probably the one person who has seen the cracks in Caleb’s very carefully built persona and never judged him for it. 

“I’m fine.” Caleb says, quietly, but Paul doesn't stop. 

“Have crowds become too much for you these days? You’ve been convincing everyone that you’re fine since the attack, but I’ve noticed the way you’ve started avoiding them” 

Always so perceptive, damn you Paul. 

The words settle between them, Caleb’s finger tighten slightly around the edge of the railing. 

“Paul…”

“I know you don't want everyone fussing over you.” His voice remains gentle. “But you don’t have to pretend with me.” 

Caleb swallows. “I wasn’t pretending.” 

Paul gives him a look, that says he’s not fooling anyone and that causes Caleb to sigh. 

“Alright. Perhaps a little.” Caleb turns around and leans back against the stone ledge, tilting his head up into the sky. “I just…I don’t know, I suppose I haven't quite shaken it.” 

Paul nods slowly. 

“It comes back to me in fragments, sometimes it feels like I am back there again surrounded by gun fire.” 

“It must be a lot for you.” 

Caleb gives a small shrug. 

It’s true, that there are nights when a sudden sound outside his bedroom makes him sit upright before he’s even fully awake. Nights, when his mind drags him back to the museum, the gunshots, the screaming and the darkness of the stairwell. 

And then there was the stranger, the only person in the chaos of that night who had managed to keep him grounded. Perhaps that was why Caleb couldn't seem to get the man out of his head. When he had been certain he was going to die, the stranger had somehow made him feel safe. 

Caleb hates how his mind always returns to him. 

He glances sideways at Paul. 

“Can I ask you something?” 

“Of course.” 

Caleb hesitates, but then continues. “Do you think, it’s possible to meet someone only once ... .really only once…and somehow have that person stay with you?” 

Paul doesn’t answer immediately. Caleb gives a quiet, embarrassed laugh. 

“That sounded much less ridiculous in my head.” 

“No.” Paul says. “I think I understand. ” 

“You do?” 

“My wife.” He says, smiling gently.. 

Something softens in Caleb’s expression. “Right…” Caleb chuckles. “You never told me how you two met.” 

Paul laughs. “It’s a rather embarrassing story.” And Caleb leans against the railing silently implying that he’s listening.

Paul gives in. “Alright then, we met at a train station. We were on different trains. They stopped beside each other, and I happened to look out the window.” 

“And?” 

Paul lets out a sigh as he stares into the distance, a soft smile on his face, reliving probably one of his fondest memories. 

“And she was sitting right across from me with a book.” 

Caleb smiles. “That sounds romantic.” 

“It wasn’t, I was staring, quite shamelessly, actually.” 

“Ah, that makes sense. At least you're honest.” Caleb laughs. 

“Her scarf slipped out the window, and I tried to rescue it.” Paul says with a sheepish grin. “I ran all the way down to the platform, picked it up and ran back to give it to her.” 

Caleb’s eyes go wide. “Paul…you ran across an entire train platform for a woman’s scarf?” 

“It does sound ridiculous, doesn’t it? I wasn’t thinking clearly. I handed her the scarf and she looked at me as though I’d lost my mind.” 

“Very reasonable, you see a pretty lady and pretty much wants to run across the train station to court her.” Caleb laughs. 

“Yeah well, then my train started moving and I wasn’t on it, so, like a fool I started running after it and she saw everything and probably thought I am a complete lunatic” 

Caleb bursts into laughter. “Wait, so you missed your train because you were staring at a woman. Paul, you're hopeless.” Caleb is still laughing, his hand resting on Paul’s shoulder, while Paul just stands there with his cheeks red and a sheepish smile on his face. 

“I spent the next year trying to find her, and then one day she showed up at a little diner, after that I decided I wasn't going to let her disappear again.” 

Caleb watches him fondly, there’s something painfully beautiful about seeing that soft expression on Paul’s face. Something stirs in his heart and then he briefly realizes how he came so close to doing something as foolish as Paul today. 

Running after some unknown stranger because he looked familiar. Whatever in the world he was trying to achieve by doing that? 

“That’s got to be the most romantic thing I have ever heard.” Caleb tells him. 

Paul shrugs. 

"Love makes people do stupid things."

Caleb's smile linger and his mind betrays him immediately, before his thoughts wander off to the stranger, he looks away. 

And then, Paul’s next question catches him off guard. “Have you ever been in love, Your Highness?” 

Caleb’s head lifts. Four months ago, he would have laughed, and the answer would have been effortlessly, a no. 

Now, however, the word refuses to come. 

“I don’t know.” 

Paul nods, as though that answer makes perfect sense. 

“I suppose you don’t always know until it happens.” 

“What happens?” 

“You stop being able to control it. You don't plan for it, you decide you're going to fall in love with someone. One day, they are just simply there, and then suddenly they are everywhere.” 

Clabe’s throat tightens. 

“That sounds terribly inconvenient.” 

Paul smiles. “It is.” 

Caleb laughs softly. “And what happens when they are not there?” 

Paul’s expression becomes thoughtful. 

“You miss them.” The answer hits Caleb harder than it should have. He goes completely quiet and stares down at the ground. 

How ridiculous it is that Paul is saying exactly the same things he feels right now for a stranger and calls it love. Because that man saved his life, and somehow, impossibly, Caleb misses him. 

Paul reaches over and gives Caleb’s arm a gentle squeeze bringing him out of his thoughts. 

“You’ll find your person eventually, whoever she is.” Paul says. Caleb glances up and raises his eyebrows. 

Paul lets out a chuckle and quickly corrects himself. “Or he.” 

A smile finally breaks through Caleb's expression.

"Better."

Paul chuckles. “Let’s keep this particular detail between us.” 

“Oh agreed. I’d rather not have my father summon the entire Cabinet to discuss my love life.” 

They share another laugh and for a little while, the world feels normal again.  

Just then comes a soft knock. 

Paul quickly straightens, when a royal guard steps into the room. 

“Your Highness, His Majesty requests your presence in the Great Hall.” 

Caleb’s smile disappears. 

“Of course he does.” Caleb straightens his shirt, and runs a hand through his dishevelled hair to tame them down a bit. “I guess I am in for another lecture for skipping lunch.” 

“Think you’re gonna need some good luck?”

“Yes, thank you Paul” 

Although he wonders why his father is calling him to the main halls, usually it’s over dinner he gets an earful, but he doesn't think much of it and follows the guard outside. 

++++

Caleb immediately feels something is wrong. 

The main hall is too quiet, usually, this particular room is always alive with movement. Staff crossing through adjoining corridors, guards standing at their posts, advisors coming in and going out, folders tucked beneath their arms. 

Tonight, there is none of that. 

Only the King, and Matilda wait for him. 

Several royal guards are stationed around the room, but something catches Caleb’s eye. Near the entrance, two men stand apart from the others, dressed in attire distinctly different from the royal security uniform.

They stand perfectly straight, dark glasses concealing their eyes, their hands clasped neatly in front of them. Small Bluetooth earpieces sit discreetly in their ears, the crisp black suits making them look more like private security than royal guards.

Caleb has never seen them before.

He stops just inside the doorway as he passes them by. 

Brilliant, his first thought is to ask whether someone had died. 

The second is to turn around and leave, unfortunately Matilda sees him before he could do either.  

“There you are.” 

Her voice is gentle, as she smiles at him. Caleb smiles back as he walks toward them. 

“You both look terribly serious. Should I be concerned, or have I finally managed to become the subject of a family meeting?” 

His attempt at humour earns him a small smile from Matilda, but the King says nothing, his expression remains firm. 

That’s when Caleb’s amusement fades. 

Matilda gestures toward the chair opposite them. “Come and sit, Caleb.” 

He does, though not without glancing between them.

“You look like you’re about to drop some terrible news on me.” Caleb says cautiously, looking straight at Matilda. 

She gives him another sympathetic smile. 

“Caleb,” Matilda starts. “We need to talk about the Paris attack” 

The smile disappears from Caleb’s face, he leans back. 

“What about it?” 

“We’ve gone over the official report several times,” she says. “The security team recovered most of the sequence of events. They know where you were when the attack began, and they also know how you reached the exit.” 

Caleb remains silent, Matilda watches him carefully. 

“But there is one part, you very intentionally skipped.” 

Caleb’s pulse stumbles. 

“The man who got you out.” 

Everything inside Caleb goes still. For one horrifying second, he wonders whether his face has betrayed him. And suddenly he’s forcing himself to breathe. 

“I don’t know what you mean.” He keeps his voice steady, trying hard to not let Matilda see past his wall.  

Matilda’s expression softens. “Caleb…”

“I was separated from the guards during the attack, someone helped me find an exit, it’s not a big deal.” 

“Someone?” 

Caleb shrugs, hand squeezing the handle of the chair. “Presumably a security personnel.” 

“Caleb, there was another man.” Matilda says, leaning forward silently, tone gentle trying to get through him. 

Caleb’s heart drops, as though something has physically fallen through his chest and landed somewhere beneath his stomach. 

They knew, and they are just trying to coax it out of him now. 

All this time, the one thing he had kept entirely to himself, the one strange, impossible memory he had been guarding as though it belonged solely to him. 

But it’s not his secret anymore. 

“How much do you know?” 

The question slips out before he could stop it. Matilda exchanges a brief look with the King. 

“Enough to know he wasn’t part of your official security detail. And we believe he may have saved your life.” 

It all comes back to him again like always, the darkness, gunshots and the strong hand around his wrist. The stupid fucking hat that has spent four month hidden at the back of his drawer because he couldn’t bring himself to throw it away. 

Caleb lets out a short breath. “I didn't think it was important,” he says quietly. 

Matilda’s expression tells him she knows that isn’t entirely true. 

“Perhaps not.” she leans back. “But the circumstances surrounding the attack have become considerably more complicated.” 

Caleb raises an eyebrow. “How complicated?” 

His father answers this time. 

“The man who attempted to kill you belonged to one of the four powerful Sicilian mafia families.” 

The room suddenly feels colder. Caleb blinks once, twice. 

“You cannot possibly be serious?” 

“Caleb…” Matilda speaks but is quickly cut off. 

“You thought that was something I needed to hear casually over an evening conversation?” 

“We just wanted to be sure of ourselves first.” 

“No, I am just trying to understand how we have gone from someone who shot at me in Paris to Italian organized crime wanting me dead.” 

Matilda’s expression remains composed. 

“Which is precisely why we need to take this seriously.” 

Caleb leans back into his chair, almost slumping and lets out a tired sigh. “This is wonderful.” 

“There is more.” 

Of course there is. 

“You have two diplomatic assignments in Italy coming up.” 

Caleb nods. “I know.” 

“Your visit to Sicily is particularly important. Under normal circumstances, we would have relied on your existing security detail.” Caleb gives another nod urging her to continue. 

“But now, we are adding someone.” 

Caleb stares at her. 

“Someone, you mean another body guard?” 

“Someone who understands the people you’ll be dealing with, from the inside.” 

Caleb is not dumb, he can put the pieces together and know exactly what Matilda is talking about. 

“You want to put a mafia operative on my security detail?” 

Caleb notices Matilda is looking nervous now, as she presses her folded hands together. 

“Not quite.” 

“And that sounds considerably worse.” 

“The Conti family has agreed to assist us.” 

Caleb stares at her. He has heard the name before, everyone has. 

The Conti family is not merely a criminal organization whispered about in dark corners. They are old money, old power, old blood. Their influence reaches far beyond Sicily, and their reputation has survived generations of governments, rival families and shifting alliances. 

Caleb rubs his forehead. 

“I have enough guards.” 

“This isn't about numbers, it’s about proximity.”

“Proximity?” Caleb questions. “What, you want someone following me around every second of the day?” 

“The person won’t be your ordinary bodyguard.” 

“That is hardly reassuring.” 

“Caleb you need to understand….” but then Matilda is cut off as footsteps are heard from a distance. 

Slow, measured. Coming from the corridor beyond the halls. The guards standing near the entrance, the same guards in suits, straighten up.
