The King turns his attention as well, Matilda says nothing as she also stands up from her chair. Caleb glances up as the footsteps grow closer. And then enters the hall. 

Caleb forgets how to breathe. 

A man walks into the hall. Tall and impossibly composed. 

Dressed perfectly in a tailored black suit, that seems to absorb light around him. His dark hair swept neatly away from his face. 

No baseball cap. No leather jacket. No careless shadow obscuring him. 

Nothing hidden or accidental, because the man in front of Caleb looks like he belongs here. 

His expression is cold and unreadable, his shoulders straight, and his movements calm. 

He stops right in front of the three members of the royal family, eyes on the King. 

But Caleb doesn’t look anywhere else, because he knows that face. Not from the photographs, or newspapers. 

From memory. 

His heart begins pounding, and he doesn’t even care about the painful expression he’s wearing right now. 

No. 

It can’t be. 

His mind rejects it before his heart ever has a chance.

Then the man’s eyes lift to the side, and meets Caleb. 

There it is. 

The same intense gaze, only now there is nowhere to hide from it. Caleb feels the blood drain from his face. 

It’s him. The man who racked up his brain for months, whose voice has followed him into his dreams. This same man he’s been secretly, stupidly, hopelessly searching without even knowing his name, is now standing right in front of him. 

Matilda’s voice comes from somewhere beside him. 

“Caleb, I’d like you to meet Arian Conti.” 

Caleb doesn’t hear anything after that, because Arian is already looking at him, and there’s a faintest flicker in those dark eyes. 

Recognition. 

Not surprise, or confusion. 

He had known, of course he had known. 

Caleb’s stomach twists unpleasantly, as he stares into the eyes of the stranger who has never been a stranger at all. And somehow, that hurts more than the assassination attempt ever had. 

Arian inclines his head. 

“Your Highness.” 

The voice comes out smooth, and deep, and Caleb’s entire body goes cold. 

For several minutes they just stare at each other. 

And then…Caleb smiles. 

It’s a smile he wears whenever something hurts too much to show. 

“Well…that’s certainly one way to ruin a perfectly pleasant evening.” 

Arian’s expression barely moves, but Caleb sees it, the small tightening of his jaw. And suddenly Caleb understands something else. 

Arian isn’t pleased to see him either. That realization should make him feel better, but it doesn’t, not even a little. 

Finally the King is the first to move from his chair, extending a hand toward an empty chair beside him.

“Arian, we are happy you could join us, please have a seat.” 

Arian inclines his head, and then crosses the room with the same measured composure Caleb remembers from Paris. He takes the offered seat beside the King, settling into it as though he has every right to be there. 

Perhaps he does. 

Caleb hasn’t looked away from him once since he has walked in, not even when his father folds his hand and begins speaking. 

Caleb notices how casually his father is addressing Arian, as if they had known each other for a long time, and it twists something ugly in Caleb’s stomach. His entire body is operating at a different speed from the rest of the room right now. 

And Arian…

Arian looks perfectly and infuriatingly calm. 

Caleb finally turns toward his father. “So this is your solution?” 

The King frowns. 

“Caleb…” 

“You decided the best way to keep me safe was to assign another one of your guard dogs to follow me everywhere?” 

“Caleb, watch your tone.” 

But Caleb smiles, not particularly a pleasant smile. Matilda quickly intervenes before the King could. 

“Arian isn’t a member of your security detail, he has an alliance with us.” 

“What kind of alliance?” 

“A transactional one.” Arian speaks suddenly, voice calm, composed and deep. Caleb’s eyes shift to him. 

Then Arian leans back. “The Conti family has longstanding influence throughout Sicily and extensive connections with the other major families. I can provide the royal family with information and protection that your own security services simply cannot.” 

Caleb gives a humourless laugh. “So you’re a glorified informant” 

And something changes in Arian’s face, but Matilda intervenes, standing up to clear the tense air. 

“No, he’s not. He’s the Godfather of the Conti Family.” 

The words land heavily, and perhaps for the first time that evening, Caleb has absolutely nothing to say. 

Godfather.

The word carries a weight that needs no explanation. He looks at Arian again, a thought occurring to him for the first time. How old is he? He certainly doesn’t look old enough to hold a position like that.

And yet, the title says otherwise.

The gravity of the knowledge slowly sinks in. To become Godfather at an age like his means Arian must have earned his place through bloodshed. He is powerful. Dangerous. And far more ruthless than Caleb had initially realized.

“You brought a mafia godfather into the palace.” 

“We brought in someone capable of protecting you,” the King says. 

Caleb laughs in disbelief. “From criminals, yes. And your solution to protect me from criminals is to assign me one?” 

His eyes return to Arian and he deliberately lets the word hang between them. Arian doesn’t react. 

The King leans forward.

“You don't seem to understand the seriousness of the situation.” 

“No, I understand perfectly, you say it is a form of safety measure for me but it’s actually about protecting the Crown’s reputation.” 

“Caleb…” Matilda starts. 

“Isn’t it? Everything always comes back to the Crown.” 

“Caleb that is enough out of you.” 

Caleb ignores his father, he can't stop now. Not with Arian sitting there watching him, not when the truth is suddenly becoming unbearable.

“Tell me, Father,” he says quietly, “have you never considered what happens when you keep a wolf on a leash for too long?” 

The room is silent, as Caleb smiles thinly. 

“Eventually, it stops remembering who put the collar on it.” 

“Caleb please…” Matilda’s voice comes out softly, almost brokenly. 

Arian moves. The scrape of his chair against the floor is quiet, but it immediately draws everyone’s attention. 

Caleb’s eyes snap toward him. 

Arian rises to his feet, and for the first time since entering the room, something shifts in his expression. 

It’s definitely not anger, but something colder, and controlled. His gaze remains fixed on Caleb. 

“Allow me to speak, your Majesty.” 

The King gives a small nod and Arian’s eyes are on Caleb again. Caleb suddenly understands why people fear this man. It isn’t because he has threatened people before, or belongs to a powerful mafia family. 

It’s the controlled calm with which he stands and commands the entire room, completely comfortable even though he’s surrounded by the people who are supposed to outrank him.  

“When I agreed to this arrangement,” Arian says, “I did not agree to become a servant of the Crown.” 

Caleb’s expression hardens. Arian continues. 

“The Conti family provides the Crown with access to territories, intelligence and alliances that would otherwise remain beyond its reach. And in return, the Crown provides us with political protection where our interests intersect. That is the arrangement.” 

He looks toward the King.

“Neither side serves the other, we cooperate.” His eyes returns to Caleb, emphasizing the last word. 

Arian steps away from the chair. 

“I am not here because His Majesty commands me to be. I am here because I agreed to be.” 

Something about that makes Caleb’s chest ache, because this isn’t what he wanted to hear. 

He wanted to believe that Arian had been here for him, not because of some agreement, or the King, or because protecting the Prince was useful to the Conti family. 

But because he had looked at Caleb that night and chosen him. 

Caleb hates himself for ever thinking that. 

Arian’s voice brings him back. “And while I am responsible for your protection, I will not be treated as though I wear the Crown’s collar.” 

Caleb’s eyes narrow. Their eyes lock, neither of them refusing to look away. 

“How noble.”

“I do not require your approval, Your Highness.”  And that does it. Caleb quickly stands up, pushing the chair several inches back. 

“Don’t call me that.” 

A slight smirk forms on Arian’s face. “Then stop behaving as though the title is beneath you.” 

Caleb stares at him. The room seems to disappear around them. And then Caleb smiles back, sharp, beautiful, defensive. 

“Don’t worry Godfather, I wouldn’t dream of making your job difficult.” 

Arian’s eyes darken almost imperceptibly, the smirk still there. 

“Good.” 

Caleb’s heart stutters, because he says it in the same way he spoke to him that night, because despite  everything, his mind betrays him and takes him back to the man beneath the hat. 

“That’s enough, Caleb.” The King speaks, voice lower, as if he’s almost tired of saying the same thing over and over again. He looks at him with the kind of expression that leaves very little room for argument. 

“You will cooperate this time. I will not have you turning this into another spectacle because you dislike the arrangements made for your protection.” 

Caleb opens his mouth, but the King raises his hand. 

“This is your best option. You will follow the instructions given to you, and you will do so without causing another problem for this family. And if you refuse, Caleb, there will be consequences.”

Caleb knows better than to test that tone. 

His father rarely threatens him directly, but when he does, it means the decision is already made.

The King turns toward the guards. “See that Mr. Conti is given everything he requires.”  

Then without another word, he walks out of the room. 

Matilda is the first to move after, she approaches Caleb slowly, her expression soft as she reaches for him and places a hand against his arm. 

Caleb flinches, it’s barely noticeable, but Matilda notices it. 

“Caleb….we had no choice….” she says quietly. “I had to make your protection the priority.” 

Caleb stares at her, tiredly because he is absolutely done with everything at this point. The hurt is still sitting there, ugly and raw beneath everything else. 

“Sometimes, you speak to me in that tone and I almost forget that, at the end of the day, all you care about is making Father happy.” His voice comes out deceptive, almost cruel. 

Matilda’s expression falls. 

But he doesn’t care, he is angry now, and sometimes angry people have a remarkable talent for knowing exactly where to put the knife.

“You're just like Andrew.”

The words land as Matilda’s lips part slightly. 

For one brief second, Caleb wishes he could take the words back, but he doesn’t.

She lowers her gaze, taking the blow with the quiet dignity she seems to carry everywhere. 

“I hope one day you understand how wrong you are.” 

Her fingers give his arm one final, almost affectionate squeeze before she turns away. 

She approaches Arian, who has been watching the exchange silently. “Mr. Conti, We are delighted to make your acquaintance. Our guards will escort you to your room.” She says politely. 

Arian gives a small nod. 

“Likewise, Your Highness.” 

Matilda leaves before glancing in Caleb’s direction one last time, and suddenly there are only two people left in the room. 

Caleb and Arian. 

The silence changes immediately. 

Caleb shoves both hands into his pocket, forcing his shoulders loose. He will not let this man see him rattled, not after everything. 

“So,” he says casually looking him over. “What exactly are you?” 

Arian doesn’t blink. “Your protection.” 

“Very reassuring.” he gives a small humourless laugh. Something shifts in Arian, as a smirk appears at the corner of his mouth, and then he’s coming closer. 

Caleb’s stomach does something deeply inconvenient, but he holds his ground. He refuses to step back, even when Arian stops barely a breath away. 

Up close, he looks nothing like the man Caleb remembers from Paris. And yet, it’s unmistakably him. 

Same eyes, same controlled presence. 

The same man who had pulled him out of the chaos of the night and held his wrist while gunfire echoed through the stairwell. 

Caleb swallows, when Arian stares down at him. 

“You don’t have to like it,” Arian says quietly. 

Caleb laughs softly in his face. “Oh don’t worry, I don't." 

Neither move, as the silence stretches, and for one treacherous moment, Caleb forgets where they are. Arian is so close, he can smell that faint scent of warm sandalwood and smoke, just like back at the rooftop when his body had pressed so close to his, he felt the warmth for hours. 

And then his eyes drop, only for a second to Arian’s mouth. He catches himself just as quickly and looks back up. 

Too late. 

Something in Arian’s expression changes, and Caleb’s heart stumbles. 

He hates it

And he hates that this man is standing in front of him after four months of wondering, the mystery he had carried around like a secret has suddenly become a person. 

And most of all, he hates how much he wants to know him. 

And then, before Caleb could stop himself, speaks quietly. “You know, I spent a rather embarrassing amount of time wondering who you were.” 

Arian says nothing. Caleb’s smile falters. 

“I wondered if I’d ever see you again.” 

There, it’s out, far too honest and for the briefest moment, something flickers across Arian’s face. So quickly that Caleb almost convinces himself he just imagined it. 

He immediately covers his vulnerability with a crooked smile. “But apparently I was being far too sentimental.” 

Arian’s jaw tightens. 

“You’re simply my father’s very expensive attack dog.” 

“I already told you, I don’t work for your father.” 

“Hmm, that is a pity.” Caleb says, tilting his head. “I think you’d look rather good on a leash.” 

Arian inhales, almost as if he’s trying to calm his nerves. 

Caleb continues deliberately, enjoying the provocation now. “I could even have a little name tag made. Something tasteful.” 

Then Arian smiles, but not kindly, something far more dangerous.

He tilts his head, closing the remaining distance between them by another fraction.

“Careful, Your Highness.” Caleb's pulse kicks in. “You seem to be forgetting something.”

“And what's that?”

“I may not work for the Crown.” Arian says, holding his gaze. “But I am the Crown's appointed enforcer.”

His voice drops lower.

“That distinction matters.” Arian smirks, as Caleb’s smile fades. “The King has entrusted your protection to me. That means that while you remain under my protection, you follow the security protocols I established. You go where I tell you to go. You stay where I tell you to stay.” 

His eyes narrow slightly. 

“And if I tell you to run, you run.” 

Caleb stares at him, there is no arrogance in Arian’s voice, and that is the worst part, he isn’t trying to intimidate him, he is simply stating a fact. 

Caleb doesn’t know when he has started to lose his breath, because he is trying to catch it now. “You don’t command me.” 

“No.” Arian says as his gaze doesn’t waver, they have gotten so close that Caleb has to look up to meet his gaze. “Your father does. And because your father has placed your safety in my hands, you will obey me when it concerns your protection.” 

And there it is, the loophole. The damn bastard is right and Caleb hates that he is right. 

Not being able to handle it, Caleb looks away with an irritated huff and takes a step back. 

“Obey you?” he scoffs. “I’d rather die.” 

Arian watches him for a long time, then quietly. “I suppose we’ll see.” 

Caleb looks back and Arian is already stepping away. He watches as Arian raises his one hand, signalling the two guards by the entrance who immediately approach him. They bow their heads and Arian turns toward them. 

“We’ll discuss your schedule in the morning. Don’t be late.” 

And with that he starts to walk away.

Once he’s gone, the room becomes unbearably quiet. 

Caleb’s carefully constructed composure collapses, he sinks into the nearest chair staring at the empty doorway. 

For four months, he'd wondered about a stranger.

A man he'd met for perhaps ten minutes.

A man whose hat he'd hidden in the deepest corner of his drawer because somehow possessing something that belonged to him had made Caleb feel less alone.

He imagined a hundred different possibilities. He never imagined this.

A mafia godfather.

His father's secret weapon.

His new protector.

Caleb leans back and closes his eyes, trying to make the tears go away but he is too overwhelmed at this point to stop.

“Fuck.” For the first time, there is no sarcasm in his voice. 

Only heartbreak.

Chapter End Notes

Hi Guys, I just want to thank you all for giving this fic a chance. I’m just way too obsessed with Caleb and Arian right now, and this is the only way to express my love for the things I enjoy.

Also, I am sorry if you come across typos, I don’t have a beta reader, so I just proofread it all myself.

Hope you guys enjoy this chapter!

Chapter 4: Chapter 4

Chapter Summary

The chapter opens in Arian’s POV, following the events of the polo match and leading up to his eventual meeting with Caleb.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

The prince has always been beautiful. That is hardly an observation. 

The Whitmores have spent generations being photographed, painted and discussed as though physical beauty is another hereditary privilege of the Crown. 

Caleb simply happens to attract more attention than most. 

Back in Paris, Arian had seen it with his own eyes. The way Prince Caleb carried himself with confidence, how the crowd had surrounded him all evening, young women, men twice his age, and even some elderly patrons who waited for the chance to exchange a few words with him. 

That kind of grace is useful for a prince. 

What Arian had not seen until this afternoon was Caleb’s horseback riding skills. 

He has just returned from the polo grounds less than an hour ago. But apparently, not all of him has left.

Arian watches through his bedroom window, as the last of the evening fades beyond the grounds of his London residence. His jacket is discarded across the back of a chair that he doesn't feel like putting on again, but he needs to be in a meeting with other leaders in less than an hour. 

His thoughts keep returning back to the prince. 

The Prince had been different on that field.

Different from the night when he had been frightened and Arian felt it in the hand, gripping his arm whenever gunfire sounded too close. But despite the fear he still kept his head, still helped people escape, fought Arian when he believed him to be a threat, and somehow, in the middle of an assassination attempt, still found the audacity to flirt with him. 

Today however, he looked entirely at ease, not to mention he rode well.

Riding has always been an unusual skill among British aristocracy, and Caleb has apparently been doing it since childhood. 

But there was that streak of recklessness that came with it, one that the Prince is known for. 

Caleb rode as though the possibility of falling had never occurred to him. At one point his older sister had stolen the ball directly from him and Caleb had shouted something after her. Judging by the look Matilda Whitmore had thrown over her shoulder, it had not been particularly princely. 

Arian had almost smiled. 

Caleb is not only reckless, he enjoys the attention as well. 

Every time he made a score, he raised his mallet in triumph, laughed at something one of his siblings shouted from the sidelines, his face flushed from exertion, and dark hair thoroughly ruined by the wind.

Arian had watched, he had been there to observe, that was the point. 

Caleb’s schedule over the coming months will put him in environments far less controlled than a private royal polo ground. Arian needs to understand how he behaves in crowds, how much attention he pays to his surroundings, whether he follows instructions, whether he is capable of recognizing danger before it reaches him. 

But judging by how he was foolishly trying to save others in an open fire meant for him, the answers so far are: poorly and selectively. 

Arian finally reaches for his drink, the whisky burns pleasantly on the way down. 

Then, there had been that moment. Caleb had looked toward the royal enclosure and Arian had seen the recognition begin before the prince could possibly have been certain. 

Even from the distance he could sense the tensed shoulder, smile disappearing, eyes narrowing. His attention had remained fixed on Arian for several seconds too long. 

It’s been four months, and he remembers. Arian isn’t sure why that surprises him. 

It was hardly the night one forgets, still when the king had turned to speak to him moments later, Arian had welcomed the interruption more than he cared to admit. 

He had left shortly after the match. 

He will see Caleb again tonight, properly. The thought has followed him home. 

A knock sounds at the bedroom door, and one of his men enters. 

“Mr. Caruso and Mr Barone are waiting downstairs.” 

Arian checks his watch and curses under his breath. They are early.

He isn't particularly thrilled to join them in the boardroom tonight, but the meeting has been called for the sole purpose of discussing the predicament he has managed to rope himself into. Word travels quickly among the mafia families, and transparency has always been one of the few non-negotiable terms of their alliance.

Arian understands their scrutiny. Becoming the youngest Godfather among them had earned him more than his share of doubt over the years. Some still considered him reckless, others dangerously unpredictable. He had long since stopped caring about it. 

“I’ll be there in five minutes.” 

The man nods and leaves as Arian finishes his whisky.

Whatever thoughts he'd brought home from the polo grounds could wait.

He has business to attend to.

++++

Vincent Caruso is already drinking when Arian enters the room. 

Enzo Barone stands near the fireplace, examining one of the paintings. Neither bother with formalities. They all have known one another too long to waste time on that. 

“You look thrilled,” Vincent says. Arian takes his seat behind the desk. 

“I am always thrilled to see you.” Arian replies in a straight tone. 

Enzo chuckles as he joins them on the table, taking a seat beside Vincent. “Careful now, he might think you mean it.” 

Arian gives him a look but says nothing. Out of the two, Enzo Barone is perhaps the only one who can afford the privilege of calling out Arian in the middle of a meeting and get away with it. The man is well into his sixties, he has been a fixture in Arian’s life long before Arian ever became Godfather. 

When his father and brother died, leaving behind a fractured empire and far too many men eager to test the young Conti who inherited it, Enzo had been one of the few who stayed close, offering counsel when Arian needed it and knowing when to step aside when he didn’t.

“As much as I appreciate you Uncle Enzo, barging into my home the moment you heard I was in London, is already beginning to feel like a spectacular waste of my time.” he says as he grabs the bottle of whiskey and starts to pour himself a drink.

Enzo’s smile widens. “There he is.”

Vincent lifts his glass. “Always a pleasure being acquainted with you, Arian, which reminds me, you should call my daughter soon, Valentina thinks you’re avoiding her on purpose.” 

Arian says nothing and continues to drink from his glass. 

Enzo speaks. “Well now that we’re here, we can get to business. We heard from Palermo this morning, a few names moving around more than usual, the royal family specifically keeps showing up.” Arian doesn’t give any reaction but sits back into his seat. 

 “And while we are watching Sicily, apparently, you’re watching the Prince.” Vincent says. 

“How are you suddenly letting the Royal Family get involved in our matters, and why did we have to hear it from somewhere else and not directly from you Arian?” 

Arian looks at Enzo before reverting his gaze back to his glass. “The Crown has offered me a preposition I cannot refuse.” 

“And the preposition being the head of the Conti family personally following one of their sons around Europe?” 

“I’m not following him around Europe.” 

Enzo’s mouth twitches. “You spent three hours at a polo match today.” Again, Arian gives no reaction but he knows the bastard can read him like an open book sometimes. 

Vincent clears his throat, leaning back into his seat. “What I don't understand is why the Crown requested your protection for Caleb rather than Prince Andrew. Surely the future King presents the greater security concern.” and then his mouth twists dismissively. “Instead, they’ve handed you the reckless spare whose greatest contribution to the monarchy seems to be looking pretty for the camera and keeping the tabloids entertained.” 

Arian’s fingers tighten around his glass. And then a cruel little smile settles across his mouth as he tilts his head a little. 

“And yet, the reckless spare has managed to earn the loyalty of an entire country without a crown on his head.” he leans forward. “I’d be careful about confusing scandal with insignificance, Vincent. It makes you sound stupid.” 

Vincent’s amusement fades. 

Across the table, Enzo says nothing, though his eyes linger on Arian for a moment longer than necessary.

“So what exactly is the purpose of this?” Enzo asks, breaking the tense atmosphere. 

“The prince has two diplomatic assignments in Sicily over the coming months. One of them keeps him there for an extended period. Whoever targeted him in Paris had connections reaching into territory the Crown doesn’t understand.” 

“But you do.”

“Yes, the Crown can surround Caleb with a hundred armed men,” Arian continues. “It won’t make a difference if they don’t know who they are looking for.” 

Vincent studies him. “And in return?” 

“Political cooperation, fewer obstacles to our legitimate interests and access where we need it.”

“Legitmate interests.” Vincent scoffs. 

“Would you prefer I put crime in the agreement?” 

“At least it would be honest.” Vincent rolls his glass slowly between his fingers. “And the King trusts you with his son?” 

“He trusts the arrangement.” 

Which seems to make sense to both Enzo and Vincent. Men like them rarely build alliances on trust, it’s the mutual interest that lasts considerably longer. 

“Still seems like an extraordinary amount of trouble for a spoiled prince.” 

“Caleb is part of this arrangement, I don't intend on exposing him to any of our internal business matters, but should it come to this, you will treat him with respect.” 

Enzo finally sighs and leans forward. “Then we have an understanding. Our alliance remains as it has always been, we stay out of each other’s affairs and offer support when it is needed.” His gaze settles firmly on Arian. “But Arian,.... if the royal family interferes with our business, there will be a war they’ll have to answer for.”

Arian holds his gaze and nods once. He understands, because Enzo has never been a man to make threats he doesn’t intend to keep.

The conversation moves on. By the time Vincent and Enzo leaves, night has settled fully over London. Arian remains in the study for several minutes before he goes back to his room and changes into his suit. 

It’s almost time to head back to the palace and finally meet the Prince in person. 

By the time he's there, he realizes one thing. 

Arian has faced far worse. He has stared down men who wanted him dead and killed more than a few corrupt bastards in cold blood without losing sleep over it. Yet, nothing quite prepared him for stepping into the Great Halls of the royal palace and seeing the look on Caleb’s face, as though someone had personally kicked his puppy and stolen his horse for good measure.

And fuck, Arian had wanted to wipe that expression from his face immediately.

Instead, he had stood there in silence and watched the prince’s carefully constructed version of Paris crumble right before his eyes.

++++

Present

Caleb has decided that Arian Conti is considerably easier to tolerate from a distance. 

Three days since the fiasco in the main hall and unfortunately, the man seems determined to make everything difficult. 

Caleb is sitting beside Eleanor on one of the benches overlooking the training grounds, riding gloves discarded between them after their afternoon session. The horses are already led back towards the stables, leaving the grounds relatively quiet under the warmth of the afternoon sun. 

Across the field, a little distance away, stands the newest complication of Caleb’s life. 

Arian is surrounded by members of Caleb’s security detail, speaking to them with the calm authority of someone who has apparently walked into the palace for only three days and decided everyone should listen to him. 

Which irritatingly, they were. Caleb’s frown deepens. He can’t hear what Arian is saying from here, probably explaining the various ways one might be murdered in Sicily. 

Perhaps there are diagrams,  Caleb hopes there are, because if he is going to have his life commandeered by a criminal organization, he would at least appreciate a professionally prepared presentation. 

Beside him, Eleanor follows his gaze. 

“So,” she says after a moment. “He is your personal bodyguard now?” 

“No.” he replies, frown deepening, eyes never straying from Arian. 

“The sort who waits outside the bathroom while you bathe.” 

Caleb gives her a deadpan expression, Eleanor is watching him with an expression of such innocent curiosity that he immediately knows she is enjoying this immensely. 

“He is not my bodyguard.” 

“Hmm, father has brought in a remarkably handsome Italian mafia boss whose sole responsibility appears to be keeping you alive. I can see why you’re devastated.” 

Caleb closes his eyes. “Eleanor, I would appreciate it enormously if you stopped speaking.” 

“I imagine you would.” the woman has the audacity to cackle at his misery. 

When Caleb opens his eyes again, Eleanor is still looking at Arian. 

A very small part of him dislikes that for reasons he is choosing not to investigate. 

“Where does Father even find these people?” Eleanor continues. “He spends most evenings complaining about parliamentary dinners and then suddenly produces a Sicilian Godfather from somewhere.”

“I’ve learned not to ask.” 

“He’s always full of surprises.” 

A brief silence passes before Eleanor leans back on the palms of her hands and speaks again. 

“This is him, isn’t it?” 

Caleb knows immediately what she means, but he keeps his attention firmly on the field. 

“I don't know what you’re talking about.” 

“The man who got you out during the attack.”

“Eleanor…”

“Come on, you think I am going to let this go? A mysterious stranger who somehow got you out of the museum full of armed men and whom you conveniently neglected to mention afterwards.” 

Caleb scoffs and looks away. “I did mention him.” 

“You said someone helped you, you failed to mention that someone looked like that.” 

Caleb finally looks at her. “I wasn’t aware his appearance was relevant to the investigation.” 

“Well I am looking at him right now, clearly it’s relevant enough.” Then she narrows her eyes with sudden interest. “Wait a minute….”

Caleb doesn't like that look, it’s the look she gives when she’s about to say the most unfiltered nonsense that he doesn’t have the patience for at the moment. 

“You knew him.” 

“What?” 

“Like you genuinely knew him, like you had a moment with him, or talked to him.” 

“I met him for approximately fifteen minutes.” 

“And then said nothing about him for four months.” 

“There was nothing to say.” Caleb defends. 

Eleanor stares at him like she knows he’s bullshitting. Caleb shrugs. 

“I barely remembered him.” 

His own mind calls him a liar so loudly it’s almost offensive. Like he didn't spend four agonizing months wondering who the stranger had been. 

Barely remembered him, yeah right. 

“Hmm…right, your face says otherwise.” 

“I’m not having this conversation anymore.” 

He looks away, and his gaze lands on Arian again, just then Arian looks over as well, directly at him, their eyes meet. 

Caleb’s stomach performs an entirely unnecessary little turn. 

He looks away at once. 

Beside him, Eleanor makes a small sound.

“Stop it.” Caleb warns. 

“What? I didn't say anything.” Caleb frowns at her 

Eleanor laughs softly and rests her shoulder against his. For several blessed seconds, she allows the matter to drop.

"I'm not going to lie, Caleb. He does look dangerous." Caleb follows her gaze despite every intention not to.

"He's a mafia boss, Eleanor. I imagine he's done one or two questionable things."

"One or two?"

"Perhaps several."

"Father clearly has excellent judgment."

“Or he’s really in some deep shit to be joining hands with a mafia family.” Caleb mutters. 

Eleanor considers this. "I find the entire situation rather entertaining."

"Of course you do."

"Who would have imagined Father would eventually require the head of a crime family to keep our little Caleb under control?" She reaches over and pats him on the head.

Caleb recoils immediately, batting her hand away. "Would you stop doing that?"

Eleanor laughs. "Hey, I am older than you."

"By an entirely unimpressive margin. And you're becoming intolerable."

"And yet you adore me." She slips her arm through his anyway, settling comfortably against his side as though Caleb hadn't just insulted her.

"Don't you have a piano lesson?" he asks. "Surely Europe would be devastated if its resident musical prodigy failed to prepare for her next showcase."

Eleanor has played the piano since childhood and somewhere along the way has developed an intense love for music and performing. She was able to build a reputation by performing at showcases across Europe and has earned recognition beyond the usual fascination surrounding the Whitmore name. 

Caleb has always admired that about her. 

Eleanor shrugs.

"I finished this morning, and besides, this is considerably more entertaining for me."

"What is?"

"Watching you unravel over an attractive mafia boss."

Caleb gives her a flat look. “I am done with you.” he gives her a slight push in an attempt to budge her off but she sticks to him with a grin. 

“Don’t think you can pretend with me, you’ve looked at him for like the sixth time while we are here having a conversation.” 

"Have you been counting?"

"I became curious after the third."

"You desperately need a hobby." Eleanor grins and squeezes his arm.

Caleb rolls his eyes and looks at Arian again.

Seven.

Fine.

Perhaps Eleanor has a point. But really, what is he supposed to do when the man is standing there looking like that?

Arian is impossible to miss among the security officers surrounding him. He stands taller than nearly all of them, dressed in black shirt beneath a fitted dark waistcoat, his sleeves rolled neatly to his elbows. His arms folded across his chest, exposing tanned forearms that Caleb most certainly did not need to be noticing from this distance. 

He still hasn’t come to terms with the fact that this guy who showed up in a casual leather jacket is the same guy who's standing there looking absolutely calm, and powerful. 

There is nothing hiding him now. 

No more a nameless stranger, just Arian standing beneath the afternoon sun as though he has every right to be there. He doesn’t look like the man who has been brought in to join the security team, he looks like the man commanding it. 

Even from here, Caleb can see the quiet assurance in the way he carries himself. Arian never seems to fidget or shift unnecessarily. He listens when someone speaks, responds with measured words and somehow the entire group adjusts around him. 

Caleb finds it profoundly irritating. 

Across the field, Arian looks over again, and this time Caleb holds his gaze as the corner of Arian’s mouth lifts, barely. 

A fucking smirk. 

Caleb’s eyes narrow. Something tells him, Arian intends to make being unaffected extraordinarily difficult. 

++++

Diplomatic speeches have to be one of the most effective forms of torture invented by the monarchy. 

Caleb has been staring at the same paragraph for nearly ten minutes. 

It is both an honour and a privilege to celebrate the enduring cultural relationship between— 

He scratches out enduring. Then stares at it, then writes it back in. 

“Riveting…ugh, who even uses that word anymore.” he mutters to himself. 

The study has fallen quiet save for the occasional scratch of his pen and the low crackle from the fireplace. Several folders lay open across the desk, filled with briefings for the upcoming visit to Sicily, economic partnerships, cultural initiatives, diplomatic dinners and ceremonial appearances.  

All perfectly important and painfully dull. 

Sicily doesn’t bother him, if anything Caleb would happily disappear there for several months under different circumstances. He loves traveling, finding art galleries hidden away from tourist streets, wandering through old buildings, discovering the history of places through the things people have created there. 

It’s the diplomacy he hates.

The endless dinners, the speeches and always having to keep up appearances. It’s an exhausting political race towards a Crown he never asked for. 

Sometimes, Caleb envies his sisters. 

Eleanor has her music, she performs across Europe, building a reputation that belongs to her rather than merely to the Whitmore name. 

Cecily is still studying and regularly announces her intention to finish University, pack a suitcase and disappear around the world before anyone could saddle her with too many royal obligations. 

And Caleb has art. 

Paintings, drawings, galleries and auctions, modern pieces that make absolutely no sense until one stands before them long enough, and classical works that have survived centuries simply because, someone, somewhere has decided they deserve to. 

The charity auctions have been among the few royal engagements he genuinely loves hosting. 

And then the attack in Paris happened. 

Since then, his father has promptly banned him from hosting another one for the foreseeable future. Instead, there are more diplomatic assignments, more responsibility. 

His father has made it clear several times that he wants to prepare him for the possibility that whenever Andrew is absent, Caleb would be expected to step into increasingly significant duties beside him. 

The entire thing is ridiculous. 

Matilda would make a better ruler than either of them. 

She understands politics better than Caleb ever cared to. She actually enjoys the machinery of the Crown and has spent years quietly challenging traditions that should have died several generations ago. 

But apparently, being born a woman remains enough to complicate the obvious. 

For god’s sake, this isn't the nineteenth century anymore. The rules are stupid. 

Not that Caleb particularly feels like telling Matilda that at the moment. He is still angry with her. 

And Andrew…

Caleb frowns at the page. Andrew still hasn't returned from his assignment yet, which means he doesn't know about Arian. 

Or perhaps he does…

Caleb wouldn’t put it past his family to have constructed this entire absurd arrangement behind his back. 

He’s still lost in thoughts when a folder lands on his desk, startling him hard enough that his pen drags a line of ink across the page. 

He stares at it, then slowly looks up. 

Arian is standing on the other side of his desk. Caleb raises his eyebrow. 

“You know, doors traditionally come with a rather useful feature called knocking.” 

“I knocked, you didn’t answer.” 

“Then perhaps you should have taken the hint.” 

Arian ignores that and nods towards the folder. Caleb eyes it suspiciously.

“Your schedule for the next six months.” 

Caleb stares at the folder before letting out a small chuckle. “I’m sorry?” 

“It includes the Sicily assignment.” 

Caleb leans back in his chair still smiling. “I don’t keep a schedule.” 

“You do now.” 

Caleb makes a face, the bastard is serious. Always serious. 

“How exciting for me.” he mutters, but picks up the file. 

There is something particularly infuriating about the calmness with which Arian speaks to him, no raised voice or visible irritation no matter how much Caleb tries to provoke him. He delivers instructions as though it has never occurred to him that Caleb might refuse. 

Naturally, this makes Caleb want to refuse everything. 

He opens the folder, and the first few pages are unsurprising. Flight details, official appearances, meeting and travel arrangements. 

Then, Caleb stops. 

He reads one section, brows drawing together. 

“Absolutely not.” He's up on his feet. Arian simply stares back.

“What?” 

Caleb turns the folder around at Arian’s face causing him to tilt his head back a little. He taps on the page. 

Private engagements and unscheduled appearances: Principal protection will remain within immediate response distance during all public and private movements outside secured royal residences. Advance notice of location and attending parties will be required where reasonably possible.

“What exactly does all private movements mean?” 

“What it says.”

“So if I attend a fitting, you follow me?” 

“Yes.” 

“Photoshoots, dinners, private dinners?”

“Yes.” again with the one word response and that infuriatingly neutral expression, Caleb will slam his head into a wall. 

“What are you, my babysitter?” 

“It is for your protection.” 

“And I already have security.” 

“And they will remain.” 

“Then why exactly do I require you breathing down my neck?”

Arian steps closer and calmly takes the folder from Caleb’s hands. “Because your existing security understands royal protocol. I understand the people who tried to kill you.” 

That shuts him up. Caleb hates when he does that. 

"Your security team reacts to visible threats. My job is to make sure they don't become visible in the first place."

"And this requires following me into a tailor?"

"If the tailor wants you dead." Arian says matter of factly. 

"My tailor is seventy-three."

Arian smirks. "I'll take that into consideration."

Caleb's mouth twitches. "You're insufferable."

"So I've been told….recently."

Caleb has the distinct suspicion that is directed at him. He reaches for the folder, Arian hands it over.

"I'll read it, but if I find anything I dislike, I'll change it."

Arian keeps smirking knowingly as Caleb notices a hint of scoff in it.

"We'll see." Then he turns and walks towards the door. "Goodnight, Your Highness."

The door closes as Caleb grips the folder between his fingers, the urge to rip it up is there but he controls it. 

"Oh, I despise him."

++++

Arian has been at the shooting range for approximately twenty minutes when he hears footsteps approaching behind him. 

He feels the thud of the folder on the table almost in the same manner as he had done last night on the prince’s study table. Upon seeing Caleb standing there, he lowers down his pistol. 

“Okay, so I have conditions.” 

Arian sighs and places the pistol down on the table. One glance at the folder tells him Caleb has apparently spent the last hour waging a very colourful and deeply personal war against his security protocols. Sticky notes popping out from the seams and his handwriting scribbled across nearly every available inch of the pages. 

"Of course you do."

That seems to offend him, but he inhales. "I spent an hour reading this, and regrettably, most of it is reasonable."

Arian turns towards him, a smirk tugging at his lips as he folds his arms across his chest. He doesn’t miss the way Caleb’s gaze briefly drops to his forearms before snapping back to his face, followed by a rather deliberate clearing of his throat. 

"How disappointing for you."

"Immensely."

"And that still didn't stop you from annotating my notes?"

"I improved it." Caleb argues.

"You drew a skull beside section twelve."

"That one felt oppressive."

"It requires you to inform security before leaving a secured residence."

"Exactly, it feels like I am being kept a prisoner here, therefore it’s oppressive. 

Arian exhales a sigh, and shakes his head. This is going to take a while.

"Fine," he says. "Let's hear your conditions."

Caleb visibly brightens "Alright then, first, you are not following me on dates."

"I am." 

The brightness vanishes, and is replaced by a pout. Arian suddenly wants to look away. 

"That is absolutely non-negotiable, you cannot follow me on dates." Caleb says again emphasizing on each syllable like he’ll have an aneurysm. 

“I won't be sitting at the table.” 

“No, I imagine it, I’m having dinner with someone and you’re lurking three tables away looking like you’re contemplating where best to bury the body.” 

“I will not be three tables away. You won’t see me, and you won’t know where I am.” Arian says. 

“You do realize that’s even worse.” Caleb tells him. 

Arian can feel his patience wearing thin. Of all the clauses Caleb could have chosen to argue over, naturally, he had settled on the one that irritated Arian the most.

Not that Arian is particularly thrilled by the prospect of following the prince around on his dates either. But protocol is protocol. Wherever Caleb chooses to spend his time and whoever he chooses to spend it with, Arian is required to remain close enough to anticipate any potential threat.

“I’m not interested in who you sleep with, Your Highness.” Caleb’s eyebrows rise. “My concern is whether the person you’re with poses a threat to you. The clause says, I’ll keep my distance, and you won’t know I’m there.” 

Caleb laughs, Arian senses a hint of frustration in his voice. “You are very reassuring.” 

“Fine okay, but you remain invisible and you do not interfere.” 

“Unless necessary.” Arian replies.

“Define necessary.” 

“A threat to your safety, someone trying to kill you or attempting to drug you.” 

Caleb gives him a bewildered look. “Hmm right.” 

“Someone carrying a concealed weapon or whose background gives me a reason to believe they intend to harm you,” Arian continues. 

“Okay, okay I got it.” Caleb says, waving his hand. “Wait…what do you mean by background?” 

Arian says nothing, just waits until realization dawns on him. 

“You are not conducting background checks on my dates.” Caleb says, he almost looks horrified. 

“I am.” 

“Arian, seriously, this is too much.”

Arian stills, only slightly. It is the first time he has heard the prince say his name so casually, without Conti or Godfather wrapped around it like some deliberate provocation. Caleb doesn’t seem to notice what he has done. He is too busy dragging a frustrated hand through his hair, exhaling heavily as though the security protocol has forced him to reconsider every decision that has led him to this exact moment.

"Alright, fine." Caleb said eventually. "But I never see them, you discover something embarrassing but entirely legal, you take it to your grave and I never hear about it.” 

"Fine, next condition."

"Fine. Friends. I can go out whenever I please."

"Within reason."

"No, absolutely not. I go to clubs, private lounges, and parties. Sometimes without planning them, my social life does not require your approval."

"I don't recall asking you to seek it, you can go wherever you like. I need to know where you are."

“Okay, well you will keep your distance, twenty meters, where I can’t see you.” 

“That can be negotiable, distance depends upon the environment."

“You’re awfully vague, do you know that?” 

“A nightclub and a private dinner aren’t exactly the same security environment.”

Caleb’s mouth tightens, Arian shows a hint of smirk and he could already see how much the prince hates it. 

“Fine, but if you insist on following me everywhere, I expect discretion, surely the Sicilian underworld has taught you some sort of mafia invisibility magic.” 

Arian continues to look at him in a deadpanned expression. 

“An invisibility cloak, perhaps? Secret tunnels?”

Arian rolls his eyes and waits.

He simply watches Caleb continue, apparently incapable of allowing a thought to pass through his head without immediately sharing it with the room. There must be something nearby he can use to shut the prince up. Preferably something effective enough to silence him without doing any permanent damage.

Because, the alternative is admitting that Caleb’s complete lack of a filter is becoming strangely endearing.

And Arian would rather shoot himself.

He picks up the folder, choosing to ignore Caleb. 

There are more conditions. 

No entering Caleb’s bedroom without knocking unless there is an immediate threat. 

Reasonable. 

No interfering with his friends unless they pose a legitimate security concern.

Also reasonable.

No cancelling personal engagements without explaining why.

Annoying, but fair.

Caleb wants the right to reject unnecessary changes to his schedule, Arian agrees, provided security related changes remain his decision. Caleb refuses, they argue, and Arian eventually decides to compromise.

“Goodness this feels like you’re running a dictatorship.” 

By the end of it, the folder contains so much additional handwriting that the original document is barely recognizable. Arian will have to get a new print. 

“Anything else?” Arian finally asks. 

Caleb thinks about it, and Arian is immediately regretting asking.

“Actually, yes.” 

“Of course.” 

Caleb smirks as he leans forward against the table, closing some of the distance between them.

“I am agreeing to this because I understand why it’s necessary. Not because you ordered me to.”

Arian’s mouth curves. “If that helps you sleep at night, Your Highness.”

Then, because Caleb apparently believes he is the only one allowed to play this game, Arian leans forward too.

The surprise is immediate.

It flickers across Caleb’s face before he can hide it, his smirk faltering as the space between them narrows to something considerably less appropriate for a conversation about security protocols.

“But,” Arian adds quietly, “when your life is actually in danger, you’ll do exactly what I tell you.”

And Caleb says nothing, neither of them moves. 

For several seconds, it becomes a silent content neither seems willing to lose.

This close, Arian notices things he has no reason to notice. The small mole on Caleb’s cheek, the faint scattering of freckles across his cheekbones, the light reflecting into those deep hazel eyes that otherwise appear a dark shade of brown under normal lightning, the way his lashes lower slightly when his gaze flickers over Arian’s face. 

Irrelevant details, entirely fucking irrelevant. 

And yet Arian finds himself committing them to memory anyway. 

Caleb’s gaze drops, just briefly and Arian knows exactly where it lands. Something quiet and warm shifts between them, so delicate beneath the provocation that brought them this close in the first place. 

Caleb is the first to break it. 

He straightens abruptly, blinking once as though returning to himself.

“Fine,” he says.

Arian remains where he is for another second. His smirk returns.

“Fine.”

Caleb shoots him a look, but there is a faint flush climbing his cheeks now. Arian very graciously decides not to mention it. 

Mostly because he isn't entirely certain Caleb is the only one who needs a moment.

Then Caleb’s mouth curves, just like always he has a way of pulling himself out of anything. 

And then he extends his hand.

“Do we have an agreement then, Godfather?” 

So we are back to nicknames again. 

Arian looks at the offered hand, then at Caleb, before extending his own. Caleb’s palm is warm against his, his grip firm despite the softness of his skin. The contact lasts only a moment, perfectly ordinary in every sense and yet Arian remains strangely aware of it. 

“Until you break it.” 

“Tch, you have remarkably little faith in me.” 

Pulling his hand back, Arian pushes both his hands inside his pockets and steps back, perhaps he needs to keep a distance with the prince for now. 

“I met you during an assassination attempt and you tried to punch me.” 

"I literally thought you were kidnapping me." 

"And then you stole my hat."

Caleb freezes. 

The humor between them quietens, replaced by an awareness that hadn’t been there a second ago. 

Arian watches Caleb’s expression change, it’s subtle, the brief widening of his eyes, the way his lips part before closing again. For once, the prince who seems to have an answer for everything, has none. 

Because they both know what the hat means. 

Arian watches the faintest trace of color creep into his cheeks, then the prince turns and starts towards the exit. 

“We’re done here.” 

“Caleb.” 

Arian doesn’t know why he suddenly called the prince by his name, but he stops abruptly. Silence follows before Arian lifts the heavily annotated folder. 

“You forgot your masterpiece.” 

Caleb glances back. 

“Keep it, consider it a reminder of your limitations.” 

Arian looks down at the page where Caleb has drawn a skull beside one of his security protocols. 

When he looks up again, Caleb is already walking away. Arian exhales and then shakes his head. 

Insufferable, completely, relentlessly insufferable. 

His gaze remains on Caleb until he disappears through the doors. He doesn’t even realise there is a small smile betraying him before he could stop it as he stares at the little drawings the prince had drawn on the pages. 

This arrangement is going to be a fucking nightmare. 

Chapter End Notes

This fandom is growing so big so fast, it’s the first time I get to be a part of something from the start and I’m not complaining.

Thank you for reading!!

Chapter 5

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Caleb suspects that The Royal Palace of Brussels is specifically designed to make ordinary people feel poor. 

There is simply no other reasonable explanation for it. 

Crystal chandeliers hang low from gilded ceilings painted with scenes of gods and angels, lights scattering across polished marble floors and catching on enough gold leaf to fund a small country. Towering arrangements of white roses occupy every available surface, while an orchestra plays somewhere beyond the adjoining ballroom. 

Caleb has visited enough palaces and attended enough banquets to know exactly the kind of people who gather here. Presidents, ministers, diplomats and politicians, stand alongside aristocrats, whose family names stretch further back than some nations. 

Waiters move effortlessly through it all, balancing trays of champagne flutes and elaborate hors d’oeuvres. 

Everything is tasteful, yet everything feels suffocatingly performative. 

Caleb smiles, because he’s exceptionally good at being performative. He plays that part very well. 

His white suit has been tailored within an inch of its life, paired with a soft beige shirt left open at the collar, two fine chains catching occasionally beneath the light, despite the demands from his stylist to wear a tie, he felt like letting loose a little tonight . His hair had been styled before he left London this morning, though his curls have predictably begun to rebel before they even arrive. 

He shakes hands, remembers names, congratulates the Belgian delegation on the newly formalised diplomatic partnership with the Crown. Listening attentively while an elderly minister explains an infrastructure initiative Caleb has already read forty-three pages about on the  flight. 

And while he’s heavily engaged in socializing, his attention remains infuriatingly elsewhere. 

Because Arian Conti has disappeared. 

Caleb had seen him when they arrived. Arian stood beside the car as Caleb stepped out, dressed predictably in black, a perfectly cut suit over a white shirt, black tie sitting neatly at his throat. Tall, composed and offensively handsome beneath the entrance light. 

Then as soon as Caleb entered the ballroom, Arian vanished. Now it’s been an hour and a half and Caleb hasn’t seen him since. 

Which is exactly what Caleb had asked for. 

You won’t see me, and you won’t know where I am. 

Apparently, Arian had meant every word, Caleb has spent days insisting that if the man intends to follow him around Europe, he should at least possess the decency to make himself invisible. 

Well. 

Congratulations to him, perhaps the Sicilian underworld really does issue invisibility cloaks. 

Caleb should be delighted, instead, he keeps catching himself searching the room more times than he cares to count.  

Not consciously, just the occasional glance towards an entrance, or at the upper gallery in hopes to catch a glimpse of him. Knowing that Arian, wherever he is, is still watching from somewhere nearby, but Caleb didn't think he’d be this good at keeping himself hidden. 

Caleb takes a sip of his champagne 

It really shouldn't bother him, at all. He doesn’t give a damn where Arian is. 

A tap lands against his shoulder. Caleb turns, and stops. 

“Landon.” 

Landon Reed smiles brightly at him. 

“Your Highness.” 

Of all the people Caleb expected to encounter tonight, Landon had not been among them. 

He looks annoyingly good in a charcoal suit, with a pale grey shirt beneath, his dark hair styled neatly away from his face, and there is that smile. Warm, charming and painfully familiar. 

Caleb knows this smile, once used to be exceptionally effective on him. 

“It’s good to see you,” Landon says. 

Caleb recovers quickly enough to return the smile. “I thought you were overseas with your father.” 

“That was initially the plan, but work interfered, and father wanted me to attend tonight out of courtesy.” Landon smiles. “Although considering I get to see you tonight, I can’t say I’m particularly disappointed.” 

Caleb opens his mouth but nothing particularly useful comes out. 

Their last encounter didn't exactly end gracefully and he hadn’t seen Landon since the night he’d practically thrown himself at him while his thoughts had been consumed by another man entirely. 

The same man who has since returned to Caleb’s life and became a spectacular pain in the arse. 

His gaze drifts over Landon’s shoulder, but he stops himself. 

A far worse thought occurs to him instead. 

Has Arian investigated Landon? 

He must have, Arian had admitted as much when they negotiated the bloody security arrangement, that anyone Caleb spent significant private time with would be screened. 

Which means…

Nope, absolutely not, Caleb doesn’t want to know how Arian's background investigations are. 

“You look good tonight,” Landon says. 

And there it is, the slightly softer voice, and the lingering look, as though Landon has momentarily forgotten that there are other people in the room. 

Once, it would have sent something pleasant curling through Caleb’s stomach. Tonight, a strange heaviness settles instead, accompanied by the sudden, unbearable awareness that somewhere in this room, Arian might be watching. 

Caleb resists the urge to look, because why should he care? Arian certainly doesn’t. 

I’m not interested in who you sleep with. 

Excellent, because Caleb can speak to every man in the room, dance with every woman, take Landon upstairs and shag him against one of Belgium’s historically significant walls if he so desires. 

What Arian thinks about any of it is completely irrelevant. 

The thought makes Caleb inexplicably miserable. 

“Thank you,” he says at last. “You look rather nice yourself.” 

Landon’s familiar smile returns. “I was hoping I’d get the chance to speak to you tonight.” 

Caleb's fingers tighten slightly around his champagne flute. "About anything in particular?"

Landon steps closer, Caleb has the instinctive urge to step back. But he stays in his spot. 

“About last time.” 

“Landon…”

“Just hear me out, okay.” 

Caleb exhales but nods. Landon's expression softens

“I don't want things to be awkward between us. Whatever happened that night, we can leave it there. I know things between us have always been complicated.” he smiles faintly. 

“But I don't want to stop seeing you altogether.” Landon continues. “Even if we are not doing that anymore. I liked being your friend before everything else became involved. I’d like to have that again.” 

Despite that, friendship has never come particularly easy to them, Caleb smiles. 

“I’d like that too.” 

The happiness that immediately brightens Landon’s face makes guilt twist quietly in Caleb’s stomach. 

“Good,” Landon lifts his glass. “Then perhaps our first act of renewed friendship should involve getting you another drink.” 

Caleb laughs despite himself. “That sounds suspiciously like how we got into trouble the first time.” 

“I’m willing to take the risk.” 

“How very brave.” Caleb scoffs. 

Landon’s hand settles against the small of Caleb’s back as he guides him towards the bar. Caleb barely stiffens but doesn’t react. 

The touch has always been familiar, but Caleb is suddenly very aware of everything tonight. 

He thinks that wherever Arian is currently, he doesn’t seem interested enough to intervene, which means Landon may have cleared his screening with flying colors. 

Whatever, he shouldn’t even be thinking about Arian. 

Caleb wishes his own brain would develop similar indifference as the mafia leader.

++++

One drink becomes two, before Landon casually mentions that several of their mutual friends intend to visit the private club after the banquet. 

“It’ll be like old times.” Landon says. “Come with us.” 

Caleb hesitates, normally he would, that is precisely the sort of invitation Caleb prefers to these dreadful evenings. 

Except Arian doesn’t know. And under their agreement….

Christ.

When did his life reach the point where spontaneous drinking required notification to the Sicilian mafia? 

“I can’t tonight.” 

Landon’s disappointment is immediate, though he covers it quickly. 

“I’m guessing some royal obligation?” 

“Unfortunately, I still have half a speech to finish for my upcoming visit to Sicily, and if I arrive without one, Father may finally disown me.” 

“I highly doubt the British public would allow that.” And then Landon sighs. “Maybe next time then, let’s just settle with the champagne for now.” 

They speak for another few minutes before Caleb sets his glass aside. 

“Excuse me. I need the restroom.” 

“Of course” 

Caleb walks away but he doesn’t go towards the restroom, instead he wanders instead. He is certainly not looking for Arian. Arian is doing exactly what Caleb had asked him to do. Remaining unseen, giving him space and allowing him to socialise without a six-foot Sicilian shadow looming behind him. 

He should be pleased. 

Instead, every minute he goes without seeing the man seem to wind something tighter beneath his ribs. Perhaps it is simply knowing Arian is there but not being able to tell where. 

Yes, that sounds reasonable. 

He doesn’t exactly feel comfortable knowing he is being watched but can not for the life of him tell where he’s being watched from. This is just general anxiety of the unknown. Beyond that he doesn’t care what Arian does, who he talks to, where he goes.

Caleb nearly laughs at the absurdity of his own thoughts. 

He is halfway through one of the quieter adjoining galleries when he is stopped again. 

A Belgian greets him, then his wife. Two young women Caleb vaguely remembers from another reception approach afterward, and he spends several minutes asking about their studies and listening to an enthusiastic account of someone’s recent engagement. 

This part always comes naturally to him. 

Caleb loves to socialise, he likes people, and more importantly he likes being made feel that they matter. So he always listens, and smiles, and remembers details as much as he could. 

Eventually the crowd thins as he wanders farther from the main ballroom, the orchestra fading into distant murmur. He has just begun considering turning back when a voice calls after him. 

“Prine Caleb.” 

Caleb stops. The man approaching him looks to be somewhere in his fifties. His dark green velvet jacket would have been questionable enough without the collection of enormous gemstone rings occupying nearly every other finger. 

Caleb has never seen him before. 

The man behaves as though they are old friends. He reaches Caleb before an introduction could even be offered, seizes his hand enthusiastically and pulls him into an unexpected embrace. 

Caleb immediately goes rigid. 

The man’s palm lands against his back, too low, but not enough to create a scene over. 

Caleb steps away immediately.

”I have been hoping to speak with you all evening,” the man says, his accent thick. Caleb summons the smile he’s been trained to produce since childhood. 

“I am afraid you have me at a disadvantage.” He speaks gently. 

“Of course. My apologies.” 

The man introduces himself explaining that he is a longtime business associate of one of the Belgian ministers and owns several private establishments like clubs and bars throughout Brussels. 

“I have heard so much about you.” 

“I am sure,” Caleb replies pleasantly. 

Something about the way the man’s eyes travel over him makes his skin crawl. 

“I would be honoured to host you at one of my establishments the next time you’re in Brussels. Personally, of course, I could ensure you have a night you won’t forget.” 

Caleb’s smile tightens. 

“That’s very generous, although I don’t expect to be visiting again anytime soon.” 

“Then perhaps tonight.” 

“I’m afraid not.” 

“There is one nearby.” The man insists. 

“No, thank you.” Again, Caleb declines politely.

Although his palm is beginning to itch with the urge to smack it across the man’s face, doing so would inevitably result in yet another public scandal, one he is in absolutely no position to cause. 

And then the man laughs as though Caleb has made a joke. 

“You might reconsider. These events must become terribly boring for someone like you.” 

Someone like you. 

Caleb’s expression hardens and then his blood runs cold as the man steps closer. 

“My place is much more suited to the sort of evening you prefer. At least, judging from what the newspapers say.” 

And there it is. 

Caleb has encountered his type before. Men who mistake photographs of him leaving pubs as invitations, men who see headlines and decide they know what he would permit. Men who assume that because Caleb laughs loudly, drinks occasionally, flirts openly and refuses to live like a porcelain ornament, they are somehow entitled to familiarity he has never offered them. 

His smile disappears. 

“I think you have mistaken tabloid speculation for an invitation.” 

The man’s grin falters, only briefly. 

Caleb straightens his jacket. “Now, if you’ll excuse me, I should find my sisters.” 

He turns, just then a hand closes around his wrist. 

Caleb stops. 

For one strange second, everything inside him goes completely still. 

Then comes anger. 

“Let go of me.” 

The man’s smile has become almost cosnpiratorial. 

“Come now, Your Highness. There’s no need to be so serious. Think of it as business, you walk into my club with that pretty face, the photographers lose their minds, and tomorrow my establishment is on every front page of Europe.” 

Caleb pulls his wrist free. 

“Touch me again and publicity will be the least of your concerns.” 

That should have ended it.

Apparently the man is either drunk, stupid or possessed of the remarkable confidence afforded to mediocre men who have gone too long without consequences. 

Because the man is reaching out for him again, this time for the front of his jacket. 

But the hand never makes contact. 

Another hand appears from nowhere and catches the man’s wrist in midair. Hard. 

Caleb hears the sharp intake of breath before he fully registers what has happened. Then he sees the black sleeve, and his gaze travels upwards. 

Arian. 

For the first time all evening, Caleb can breathe properly. The realisation is almost as unsettling as the situation itself. 

Arian stands between them. 

Caleb has seen him irritated before, amused and infuriatingly composed, but he has never seen him like this. 

There is no anger on Arian’s face, perhaps that would have been less frightening. Because right now, his expression is almost entirely blank. 

His grip tightens, as the man’s face twists when Arian turns his wrist, not enough to injure him, Caleb thinks, but enough that the man immediately bends under pressure. 

“What…you’re…Mr. Conti, what are you doing here?”

So he knows who Arian is.  

Arian says nothing at first, he simply moves, one step, then two until the man backs up enough for Arian’s body to occupy the space between Caleb and the man completely, broad shoulders cutting Caleb off from view as naturally as though he’s been built for precisely this purpose. 

Caleb stares at his back, as something strange happens inside his chest. 

Arian finally speaks. 

“The prince declined your invitation.” 

His voice is quiet. 

The man’s eyes dart towards Caleb, Arian shifts slightly, the line of sight disappears. 

“He declined when you approached him. He declined when you propositioned him. He declined when he walked away from you.” Another slight turn of the wrist, Caleb thinks Arian will actually break it if he puts any more pressure to it. 

The man hisses, almost whimpers, which sends a spark of satisfaction inside Caleb. 

Arian leans closer. “So I am struggling to understand which part confused you.” 

“Please, Mr. Conti…I mean no disrespect” the man begs. 

“No?” 

“No Sir, of course not.” 

And then Arian tilts his head, a hint of smirk appears on his lips. 

“Good.” His voice somehow becomes softer. “Then there  should be no misunderstanding when I tell you this.” 

The man is trembling under Arian’s hold. 

“You will never put your hands on him again, not at any banquet, not at one of your clubs, not anywhere you happen to find fortunate enough to occupy the same room.” 

The man’s face noticeably drains of all color. 

Arian releases his wrist, for half a second Caleb thinks it’s over but then Arian calmly straightens the man’s cuffs where the grip has disturbed it. The gesture is so absurdly civil that Caleb almost forgets how dangerous the man standing in front of him actually is. 

“If you ever forget that,” he says quietly. “You won’t have the opportunity to be reminded twice.” 

There is no shouting, no dramatic threat, the silent way he controlled the entire situation is what’s terrifying. 

This terrible, effortless stillness is what makes Arian dangerous. 

The man certainly understands, as the next thing he does is scrambles away. 

Caleb realizes something, as he stares at the broad line of Arian’s shoulder, while he placed himself between him and a man who had made his skin crawl.

He isn’t afraid of him. 

Rather, he feels safe. 

He waits until the man disappears behind the corridor, before he speaks. 

“He knew you.” 

Arian turns in that moment and their eyes lock. For a minute they hold each other's gaze, Arian’s eyes running down his face as if inspecting any signs of physical harm, there are none. 

“He was afraid of you.” Caleb speaks again, trying to break the tense atmosphere. 

Arian doesn’t answer, and the amusement disappears almost quickly as it comes because there is something wrong with his expression. The danger hasn’t left it. 

If anything, now that the other man is gone, it seems to have redirected itself entirely towards Caleb. 

“You weren’t supposed to leave the main hall.” 

Caleb blinks. “What?” 

“You were briefed to remain within the secured areas.” 

For several seconds Caleb can only stare at him as disbelief arches up. 

“Are you seriously angry with me?”

Arian steps closer, causing Caleb to take a slow step back. 

“Do you value your life so little, or have you simply made a habit of wandering away from your security whenever the opportunity presents itself?” 

Oh. So this is the moment where Caleb’s gratitude evaporates. 

“I was trying to find the restroom.”

“There are restrooms inside the secured perimeter.”

Caleb stares at him. “Wonderful. Perhaps someone should have thought to mention that before dropping me inside a palace the size of a small country.”

“This isn’t funny.”

“I wasn’t joking.” Caleb counters back, with a frown now. 

Neither is Arian, that becomes increasingly obvious the longer Caleb looks at him. There is something unnerving in his stillness, his jaw set hard, one hand curdled tightly at his side. 

For one strange second, Caleb thinks it looks remarkably like fear. Not of anything, but fear for him, and the thought sends an unexpected warmth through his chest. 

Arian had been watching. He had seen something wrong and crossed whatever distance had been between them before the man’s hand touched Caleb again. 

Stood in front of him, protected him. 

“If you want me to respect the conditions we agreed upon, then you need to cooperate with me.” Arian says. 

“What exactly is that supposed to mean?” 

“It means I kept my distance because you asked me to. You wanted discretion and privacy tonight. I gave you both.” Arian’s voice remains controlled. But Caleb can hear the strain beneath it now. “If you continue leaving secured areas without warning I will have to visibly remain beside you at all times, and I won't entertain a single complaint about it.” 

“I had the situation handled perfectly well before you appeared” Caleb tries to protest. He’s seen Arian annoyed with him more times he can count but he’s never seen him genuinely angry, not like this. 

When Arian doesn’t say anything, Caleb steps forward. “I didn’t ask you to intervene.” 

“I am not doing this out of the goodness of my heart Caleb. I’m doing what I agreed to do, protecting you is my responsibility.” 

Something sinks unpleasantly inside Caleb. 

Because how stupid of him to think it was ever anything more than just a duty. For one embarrassing moment, standing behind Arian and watching that man tremble beneath his hand, Caleb had allowed himself to believe there was something personal in it. 

Caleb should know better than most that duty could look similar to affection when viewed from the right angle. 

His expression hardens. 

“Then consider your responsibility fulfilled, just go back to wherever you’ve been hiding all evening and leave me alone.” 

Caleb moves past him and barely makes it two steps, Arian’s arm shoots out. His palm lands flat against the wall beside Caleb blocking his path, causing him to almost bump into it.

Caleb stops abruptly, Arian is suddenly very close. 

“What the hell are you doing?” 

“You’re going back to the ballroom.” 

“Move your arm.” Caleb says, voice hard, but Arian doesn’t budge. 

Instead he steps closer. Caleb has seen enough men attempt intimidation throughout his life to recognize it, unfortunately for Arian, Caleb has never been particularly good at being intimidated. 

“You’re going to turn around, walk back into the ballroom and stay beside Princess Matilda until I make sure that man has left the premises.” 

“You are seriously acting insane right now, nothing happened.” 

“I need to determine how he got close enough to you without being flagged.” 

Caleb almost laughs. “That extraordinary little habit you have of forgetting that you do not own me.” 

Something flickers across Arian’s face. “I don't need to own you to keep you alive.” 

“And protecting me does not give you permission to control me.” 

“It gives me permission to remove you from an environment I no longer consider secure.” 

“Right, because some revolting businessman grabbed my wrist?” 

“Because someone I didn’t clear got you alone in a corridor.” 

The words land, shutting him up. Arian’s fist is still clenched beside him. He doesn’t know what to make of this. Arian says he’s performing his duty, but Caleb sees genuine worry reflecting in his eyes and this confusion does something dangerous to Caleb’s anger. 

So he steps closer. He doesn’t stop until barely any space remains between them, close enough to see the darker ring around Arian’s irises, the tension running through his jaw. 

One more inch and their noses would brush. Neither moves. 

“You need to calm down.” Caleb says quietly. Arian’s eyes narrow. 

“Do I?” 

“Yes.” his voice drops further. “Because you protecting me does not mean I stop being a person. You don’t get to decide where I go, who I speak to and when I leave simply because something might happen.” 

“And what if something does happen?”

“Then I listen. You tell me to run and I run right?”

Realization flickers in Arian’s eyes. Caleb may be bound to him by security protocols, but he will not blindly bend to his rules, nor will he allow anyone to control him.  

For one suspended moment, the argument seems to disappear beneath something else, Arian’s shoulder slightly relaxing and Caleb sees it, the hint of acknowledgement and respect reflecting in his eyes. 

Something in Caleb relaxes too, then he remembers he’s still angry with him. 

“I’ve had quite enough excitement for one evening. Please ask Paul to bring the car, I’d like to return to London.” 

He doesn’t wait for Arian to respond back, and turns to leave, walking straight into the ballroom, doing exactly as Arian had instructed. 

He finds Matilda near one of the tables. She gives him a questioning look when he takes his place beside her, but he offers no explanation. 

By the time Caleb glances back towards the corridor, Arian is gone.

++++

Arian has just finished reviewing the last of the security reports, as midnight falls.

The place has gone quiet around him. 

His temporary study sits in one of the private wings reserved for senior members of the royal household and their guests, far enough removed from the ceremonial rooms that the noise of the palace rarely reaches it. 

Arian sits behind the desk with a glass of whiskey in one hand and entirely too much of Prince Caleb Whitmore’s life spread out before him. 

Personal files, security reports and photographs. 

Names of people Caleb sees regularly, places he frequents, establishments he has been known to disappear into unreasonable hours. Friends and former lovers, even members of the staff Caleb is close to. People who have had enough access to the prince over the years to warrant looking into. 

Arian has looked into all of them. 

His gaze returns to the photograph lying directly in front of him. 

Landon Reed. 

Arian takes another sip from his drink. 

There is absolutely no reason for this, but something about Landon Reed ticks him off and the more he screens him, the more details about this man irritates him.

Arian reaches for the file again, despite having already read it twice. 

Twenty-eight, son of a prominent senator. Cambridge graduate. His finances are clean, by political standards, at least. No meaningful criminal associations, no unexplained travel, and no connections to anyone remotely linked to the Paris attack. 

Even his more questionable youthful decisions are disappointingly ordinary. 

A few drunken photographs here, or one speeding offence there. 

Arian closes the file. 

He has investigated men with significantly less information and found enough dirt to destroy entire careers. Landon Reed on the other hand has apparently committed the unforgivable crime of being perfectly respectable. 

The photographs beside the files adds to Arian’s annoyance. There are several of Reed and Caleb together. 

Most are press photographs, collected over last year. The two leaving a club, are surrounded by security and cameras. Caleb laughing, as Landon holds his hand through the crowd. Another catches them embracing outside some private function.
