Caleb looks at Arian’s back, searching for some indication of what he could be thinking. Everything Arian has spent more than a year trying to secure, is being offered to him on a silver platter. 

All he has to do is let Caleb go.

Before he could think better of it, Caleb leans forward and catches the back of Arian’s black vest between his fingers. The gesture is small, almost imperceptible, but his grip tightens all the same. 

He doesn’t know why he reached for him, only that the thought of leaving Sicily means losing whatever fragile, impossible thing has begun between them, and that frightens him more than it should. He hates how his fingers tighten instinctively, hates how desperate he feels in this moment, knowing what it would cost the both of them.

He waits for Arian to choose the deal.

For a moment, nothing happens, as Arian stands perfectly still. Then a quiet sigh escapes him, as some of the tension eases from his shoulders. He lifts his chin, meeting Florence’s eyes with a calm that makes Caleb’s heart stumble.

“Keep the estate,” Arian says quietly. “The deal is off.”

No one moves for several seconds. Florence stares at him as though he has spoken in a language she does not understand.  

“You can’t be serious.” 

"I have never been more serious in my life." The words land with finality. Florence's expression shifts in disbelief, fury and something close to humiliation. Slowly, the guns around them began to lower. Arian’s men do not relax entirely, nor does Arian take his eyes off Florence until her own guards have stepped back. Valentina lowers her weapon last, her face unreadable. 

Caleb remains motionless behind him, his hand has slipped away from Arian’s jacket, and he can’t seem to make himself move anymore.

Arian just gave up the estate. 

For him.  

“You have no idea what you’ve just sacrificed, Godfather.” 

Arian lowers his gun and Florence's stiff shoulders seem to relax. 

“You don’t need to tell me that.” He replies. 

“And you think he will be worth it?” she flicks her chin over to Caleb. 

Arian’s expression hardens. “Get out of my house.” 

Florence looks around at the armed men surrounding her and then looks at Arian with a nod, understanding that there is nothing left to negotiate. 

“The agreement is finished. I’ll deal with the Caruso’s directly from now on.” she says coldly. “Whatever business we had with the Contis, ends tonight.” 

Florence turns and strides towards the gates, her four men falling into step behind her. Only when the gates close behind them does the courtyard begin to open up around Caleb again. Though he barely noticed, his eyes glued to Arian’s back, trying to reconcile that the man who had spent over a year pursuing something important, just abandoned it in the space of a few seconds. 

Caleb cannot find a single explanation that makes sense. 

Arian turns towards him. 

Whatever thoughts are running through Caleb’s head all comes to a halt when he sees Arian cross the distance between them quickly. His hands are on Caleb’s shoulders all of a sudden, turning him gently towards the light. 

“Are you hurt?” 

The question is quiet, but when Caleb meets his eyes, he sees the worry beneath Arian’s composure, raw and unguarded in a way he has never seen before. For the first time, Caleb sees just how terrified Arian must have been of losing him. For a moment Caleb remembers, his eyes hold the same look from the warehouse. When he had looked at Caleb just before their lips met, as though touching him was the only way to reassure himself that Caleb was still there, still close enough to hold. 

“I-I’m fine.” 

Caleb’s voice comes out in a soft stutter, as Arian looks him over, his gaze moves across his face,hands tighten around Caleb’s shoulders before moving down his arms, checking for injuries with a focus that makes Caleb’s throat constrict. 

“Did he touch you?”

“No, Arian, I’m alright.” Arian's eyes search his face for another moment before they meet his.

Caleb swallows before he finds the courage to ask, softly. "What did you just do?"

His heart is beating so hard, he can feel it in his throat. Arian's grip on Caleb’s arms loosens a little bit but he appears to have no immediate answer. 

Caleb is about to call his name again, when Valentina’s voice interrupts.

“Arian, a word.” She stands a few feet away, her gun lowered to her side, the sharpness from her expression is gone, replaced by something Caleb cannot quite read. 

Arian closes his eyes briefly, but then lets Caleb go completely as he steps back. 

“Rowan, take him inside and make sure he’s unharmed.” He says to one of his men.

Caleb frowns. “I just told you I’m fine.” 

“It wouldn’t hurt to check, just go inside.” Arian tells him, leaving no room for argument.

Caleb recognizes the dismissal. 

His face closes off into a neutral expression as he takes a step back before Arian could touch him again. 

“Thank you, but I know my way inside.” He looks towards Rowan, who wisely gives him space to walk ahead, as Caleb turns and starts to walks away towards the villa without another word. 

He feels Arian’s gaze behind him, but he doesn’t look back. Whatever just happened, whatever Arian has sacrificed, Caleb is not going to stand here and wait for an explanation if it isn’t coming. 

Arian stares at Caleb’s retreating back, disappearing into the villa, and hadn’t missed the moment his expression had closed up and the hurt had replaced the worry. 

Arian knows that look, he has put it there himself, several times. 

“Well,” Valentina says beside him, “that went catastrophically badly.” 

Arian turns towards her. She has holstered her gun and is watching him with an expression that is almost amused. 

“It had to be done.”

“Oh, I don’t disagree. Although, father is going to be absolutely insufferable about losing that estate.”

“I’ll deal with Vincent.”

“I’m sure you will.” She looks at him again, her expression turning thoughtful. “You just realized what exactly you’ve done here right?”

Arian nods. “I understand the consequences.” 

She rolls her eyes. “I’m not talking about the bloody estate, Arian.” She retorts as an incredulous smile touches her lips. 

“You know, I spent years hoping you'd look at me the way you look at him. And it only took him a few months to make you lose your mind. Do you understand that you just made him your problem tonight?” She says it almost casually.

Arian had long suspected Valentina wanted something more from him, but he had never been able to offer her that. Instead, he had offered her a few casual encounters, some nights of intimacy when the weight of everything became too much. They had never crossed that line into anything beyond casual, and Valentina never asked him to. 

“I think you and I would have been a disaster together,” Arian says, his voice gentle with the familiarity of someone who has known her too long to pretend otherwise.

Valentina laughs. “Says the man pining after a royal prince. Now that is a disaster.”

Arian frowns at her. “You're enjoying this far too much.”

“I'm making up for years of disappointment. Let me have this.” she says, giving his shoulder a light nudge. “You can make it up to me by dealing with my father.”

“I’ll speak to Vincent, you don't have to explain the deal’s collapse to him alone.” 

“Good, because he is going to be furious, and I’d rather not be standing between the two of you when he finds out.” She straightens her coat, her expression settling back into its usual composure.  “I should go before he hears about this from someone else.” 

“I’ll call you tomorrow.” 

“You better.” She reaches up to brush a stray lock of hair from his forehead, a small, affectionate gesture that she has perhaps done a thousand times, before she steps away. 

It brings a subtle smile to his face. 

“Goodnight, Arian. Perhaps you should do something about that little prince of yours, he did not look happy walking away from you just now.” 

“You can leave.” Arian says in a flat tone, not at all impressed by her provocations, and causing her to chuckle as she walks away. 

Just as she’s gone, Arian turns towards the villa, reminding himself of a rather pressing matter, considerably more daunting than losing an estate or dealing with Vincent. Caleb had walked away from him looking as though he had been dismissed, and Arian had let him go without an explanation. 

Again.

Which is why he is surprised that by the time he reaches his study, Caleb is waiting outside the door. Whatever he had prepared to say to Caleb vanishes the moment he sees him. 

For a man who has just stared down an armed standoff without hesitation, Arian finds himself entirely unprepared for the sight of one furious prince.

Chapter 11

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Arian stops in the doorway, his gaze settling on Caleb who is there waiting for him with his arms folded across his chest, and enough fury in his eyes to set the entire Conti estate on fire. 

“It’s about time,” Caleb says. 

Arian draws a breath, and turns to walk past Caleb into his study, leaving the door open behind him. An indication that they can take this inside, he knows Caleb will follow. Sure enough, he hears footsteps and turns to find the prince entering with an expression of utter disbelief. 

“Seriously? Is that your response to everything that just happened?” Caleb asks. 

Arian turns slowly, leaning his hips against the desk and folding his arms, keeping his expression carefully composed. 

“What do you want me to say Caleb?” The answer is enough to make Caleb laugh once, without any humor. 

“Oh, I don’t know. Perhaps you could explain the extraordinary little stunt you pulled outside before sending me inside like some inconvenient child you no longer wished to entertain.” 

“So a deal collapsed and you happened to be there, this is hardly the first time I’ve lost one.” 

“I happened to be there? Oh, so we're pretending you didn't just give up something you've spent over a year trying to secure because Florence gave you an ultimatum that very clearly involved me?”

Arian looks away, his attention settling on the edge of his desk in an instinctive retreat to put some distance between himself and a conversation he has no idea how to survive. 

Caleb sees it at once. “Don't. Don’t you dare shut me out again.” 

“You think I don’t know what I gave up?” Arian asks, his voice hardens. 

“Then tell me why you did it.” Caleb steps closer, his voice faltering despite his best efforts to keep it steady. “I was standing right there, Arian. I saw what she offered you, and you walked away from it. You knew exactly what it would cost you, and you did it anyway. For me.” He swallows, his eyes searching Arian’s face. “Why can’t you just say it?” 

Why couldn’t he say it? Arian has no answer right now. He is still trying to understand what happened in that courtyard, the sheer certainty with which he chose Caleb over everything he had spent a year pursuing. None of it had mattered the moment Florence made him imagine a life in which Caleb is no longer there. 

He has spent years learning to weigh every decision, to measure what could be gained and lost, always knowing when to walk away, and when not to. Yet, when it came to Caleb, the choice was made before he had even finished considering it. 

“I knew exactly what it would cost me. But then…but then you were standing behind me, holding onto me as though you thought I might let you go, and all I could think was that I couldn’t.” 

Caleb goes still. Arian looks at him, his own uncertainty reflects in the silence between them. His heart is beating hard enough to make the words difficult, and he hates that he cannot seem to  explain something that has become so painfully clear in every other way. Arian doesn’t regret anything, he knows he’ll make the same choice again, a hundred times over. 

What frightens him is how little he has to think about it, how the prospect of losing Caleb has come to matter more than anything he has ever wanted for himself. 

“I couldn’t let you leave, Caleb.” 

For one impossible moment, Caleb thinks he hears something in those words that he has been aching to hear for months. He wants to believe that the choice he made in the courtyard is not simply an act of protection but something far more personal. 

Then he remembers himself clutching onto Arian, the desperate little gesture he hadn’t even been conscious of making, he remembers holding on as though he could somehow keep Arian from choosing the deal. 

And the hope in his chest begins to twist into something painful. 

“You didn’t have to do that,” Caleb says in a small, quiet voice. 

Arian sighs. “I did it anyway” 

“You gave it all up for me.” Caleb looks away unable to bear the uncertainty in Arian’s expression. “I never asked you to.” 

“I know you didn’t.” 

“Then why does it sound as though you wish you hadn’t?” 

The question catches Arian off guard. “What? Caleb, that’s not what I…” 

“Because I cannot keep doing this with you. I cannot keep trying to work out what I mean to you from the things you do and then listening to you explain them away as though they mean nothing at all.” 

Arian quickly straightens from the desk and takes a step closer. “I’m not explaining anything away.” 

“No? Then what am I supposed to make of this?” Caleb’s voice breaks, his frustration threatening to spill over something he can no longer conceal. “You keep pulling me close enough to make me believe that I mean something to you, and the moment I dare to ask what that something is, you shut me out. When you saved my life in Paris, when you kissed me in the warehouse… how am I supposed to know it was anything more than your duty?”

The words strike Arian with a force he cannot defend himself against. He wants to tell Caleb that he has misunderstood nothing, that the kiss meant more than Arian has ever allowed himself to admit. 

He wants to explain that he has only just begun to understand why the thought of losing him feels like something being torn out of his chest. 

But the truth is still unfamiliar in his mouth, too large to be handled carelessly. He has spent his life making decisions for other people, knowing precisely what he wants. He has never wanted anyone quite like this, with such complete disregard for the cost. 

Caleb watches him, waiting for something, anything. When Arian says nothing, his expression begins to close, the hope giving way to the familiar hurt he has grown so tired of carrying. 

“Right,” he murmurs. “I should have known better.” 

“Caleb, listen to me….” Arian takes another step forward but Caleb has already started to retreat. 

“No it’s fine.” His smile is brittle, his eyes bright with an emotion he is trying very hard not to show. “I am not going to ask you for any justifications. You’ve made your decision." 

Arian barely gets a chance to get a word in before Caleb turns on his heel and strides out of the study, shutting the door firmly behind him. He stands there motionless, staring at the closed door. Then the full weight of what he has just done settles over him, and feels something dangerously close to panic take hold. 

He finally told Caleb something real, only to let him walk away believing it was a mistake. Caleb had stood in front of him with his heart laid bare, asking for nothing more than the truth, and Arian had managed to make him feel as though he asked for too much. 

He should have told Caleb the deal had never been worth losing him. Instead, all he had managed to do is make Caleb believe there was nothing left worth staying for. 

And this time, Caleb might actually believe him. The realization hits him with sickening clarity. 

No. 

Without a second thought, Arian strides towards the door. He has spent too long allowing fear to make decisions for him, and he will not stand here and watch Caleb leave believing that he regrets choosing him. 

He pulls the door open. 

Caleb is standing just outside in the dark hallway. 

He looks startled to find Arian there, as though he had expected to remain behind the door, exactly where he had left him. His eyes are bright, the tears gathered there not quite spilling over, and his breathing is uneven. He looks furious and hurt and desperately hopeful all at once. 

Arian’s chest tightens, realizing Caleb had not gone far. Perhaps, despite everything, some part of him had still been waiting for Arian to follow. 

For one quiet moment, they simply look at each other. Arian doesn’t know how to word anything carefully enough to undo the hurt in his eyes. But Caleb is here and the thought of letting him walk away again is unbearable. 

Caleb’s breathing is shallow, slightly audible in the quiet hallway. Whatever he sees in Arain’s eyes seems to undo the last of his resolve. He has spent months trying to make sense of this man, trying to convince himself that wanting him is a terrible mistake. But looking at him now, he finds he no longer cares. 

So he moves first. 

Caleb crosses the distance between them in a rush, and Arian catches him around the waist just as he stumbles into his arms. Their lips meet with a desperation that leaves no room for hesitation, all the longing and hurt they have kept between them finally breaking loose. 

Caleb’s arms wind around Arian’s neck, his fingers catching in the hair at his nape, while Arian pulls him closer as though the last few seconds have been an unbearable distance he can no longer stand. The force of Caleb’s kiss drives Arian back against the study door, but he barely registers the impact. One hand rises to cradle Caleb’s face, his thumb brushing his cheeks, as he tilts his head and kisses deeper, answering every ounce of desperation Caleb brings to him. 

Caleb kisses him as though he has been waiting too long to do anything else, And Arian meets him with the same helpless urgency, unable to bear the smallest space between them. Arian’s hand cups the side of Caleb’s face, as he opens his mouth and allows the kiss to deepen, while his free hand fumbles with the door handle behind him. 

He manages to open the door without breaking their kiss and draws Caleb back into the study with him, keeping one arm firmly around his waist. The door closes behind them, and Arian presses Caleb against it, kissing him properly this time, with all the heat and hunger he has spent months denying. 

A small breathless sound escapes Caleb against his mouth, and Arian feels the last of his restraint give away. 

He presses into him more, pinning Caleb fully to the wood, as the kiss turns heated almost at once. Arian’s tongue flicks into his mouth without breaking the seal of their lips, and Caleb makes a delicious, helpless sound, completely surrendering to him, tilting his head and deepening the kiss. 

The moan that escapes Caleb’s mouth sends a vibration straight through Arian’s chest. One of Arian’s hands cradles the side of Caleb’s face, thumb stroking the flush already rising on his cheek. Caleb’s fingers clutch and run through Arian’s hair, gripping as though he might disappear. 

Caleb’s mouth feels intoxicating against his, every soft sound he makes chips away at the careful control Arian has worn like armour for years. He feels Caleb’s hips shift under his touch as he presses his fingers to steady him. Arian feels like a man who has finally lost all senses and does not care. 

Arian pulls away only slightly, before he flicks his tongue over Caleb’s lower lip, then trails downward, open-mouthed along the line of his jaw. Caleb’s breathing is sinfully shallow as he tilts his head to the side at once, offering the column of his throat without hesitation. Arian’s hands fumble at the collar of Caleb’s jacket pushing it open. He finds the warm skin of Caleb’s neck and presses his lips there, then lower trailing slow, wet kisses down the side of his throat. Caleb’s pulse quickens under his mouth. 

When their bodies press even closer, Caleb can feel him, unmistakably hard through layers of clothing as he inhales a sharp, shaky breath. 

“Arian…Arian, wait.” 

The words are almost like a moan, more than a protest. Arian stops at once. His lips remain pressed to the side of Caleb’s jaw for a heartbeat longer before he slowly lifts his head. 

The sight before him, nearly undoes him. 

Caleb looks absolutely wrecked, cheeks flushed, curls falling across his forehead, eyes wet and dark, beautiful in a way that steals the air from Arian’s lungs. Arian knows he probably doesn’t look much better either, his perfectly set hair have become disoriented from Caleb’s hands, collar undone, and clothes rumpled. 

They stare at each other in the sudden quiet. Then Arian leans forward and gives him a soft, slow kiss. Then another. And another. Gentle. Reverent. Caleb’s eyes flutter closed, his hands rest lightly on Arian’s shoulders as Arian cups the side of his face and kisses him as though they have all the time in the world.

They stop and rest their foreheads together as Arian waits. 

“I’m not asking you to stop,” Caleb says at last. “I just need you to listen to me for a moment.” 

Arian’s hand stills against his waist, as he nods, a subtle shake of his head to indicate he’s listening. 

“I cannot do this again if you’re going to kiss me like you mean it and then spend the next three days pretending it never happened.”

The rain is not heavy enough to send the remaining workers to seek shelter, but it has darkened the roads and the walls while the sea shifts beneath the night sky. Black water catches the glow of the navigation lights as ships signal their arrival and departure. 

Sicily tends to grow colder at this hour, the ports remain quiet as the day traffic dwindles, tonight however, the usual activities at the harbour have been interrupted by an unexpected explosion as the workers have fled the dock after the sudden attack.

But understanding that does not mean he wants Caleb exposed to any of it. Having him in his life is already becoming something of a necessity, an urgency Arian can neither reason with nor bring himself to resist. And somewhere beneath those feelings is the uncomfortable certainty that Caleb deserves someone better than him.

Someone who doesn't have blood on his hands.

But Arian is far too selfish to let him go now.

He remains in his study until the first traces of daylight creep over the mountains. At some point, he abandons his drink and turns to the security footage his team has sent over, reviewing it with frustration. 

Frame after frame reveals nothing useful. And that, more than anything, unsettles him.

Because now he knows Andrew will not stop. He will exhaust every possible avenue to have Caleb brought back to London, and Arian has no intention of letting that happen.

The first thing Caleb notices over the past two mornings is the empty space beside him. It has become a rather irritating way to wake up. 

For the better part of a week, he has grown accustomed to Arian’s warmth, to the heavy arm draped across his waist and the quiet reassurance of waking with someone beside him. Now, for two consecutive mornings, he has opened his eyes to find the other side of the bed empty and the sheets already cool, with Arian long gone. 

It is not like Arian has completely disappeared. Every night, without fail he returns to Caleb’s room, sometimes Caleb is already asleep by then, and sometimes he is already awake, reading or pretending to read while waiting for the familiar sound of Arian entering. Either way, Arian would come to him, settle beside him and pull him close as though the distance throughout the day has been an inconvenience. 

Every night, he presses soft kisses to Caleb’s neck and shoulder, his touch unhurried and tender, until the last of Caleb’s complaints dissolves somewhere in his throat. 

Caleb always falls quiet beneath the warmth of Arian’s mouth that trails down his throat that by the time Arian settles beside him, he is already half-asleep. 

But waking up the next day alone in bed is, however, becoming insufferable. 

Whatever has claimed so much of Arian’s attention seems to demand every waking hour. He leaves before Caleb has finished breakfast and returns long after dinner offering a vague assurance that he had business to attend to. Caleb understands that Arian has responsibilities but that doesn’t stop him from missing him terribly. 

Later in the afternoon, Caleb has just finished riding Nerone and is standing beside the stallion, one hand resting against the horse’s neck when he senses someone approaching. 

Arian has discarded his jacket somewhere along the way, his shirt sleeves buttoned neatly at the wrists. He looks as composed as ever, though Caleb notices that he seems to have come straight here rather than stopping inside the villa first. 

Caleb’s face brightens before he could help it. “Well, I wasn’t expecting to see you home before sunset, stranger.” He says looking Arian over with an exaggerated surprise. “Have you been dismissed from whatever mysterious business has been keeping you?”

Arian’s mouth curves. “Good afternoon to you, too.” 

“Oh don’t be charming now. I’ve spent two days having breakfast alone.” 

Arian raises his eyebrows. “Two days.” 

“Forty-eight hours Arian, I’ve been counting.” Caleb gives him an affronted look, though a smile tugs at his mouth, as Arian gives a light laugh, and reaches out to stroke Nerone’s nose. 

“I see you two are getting along rather well,” Arian remarks. 

“He’s been very good company. He listens when I speak and has the courtesy to remain in the same room as me for more than ten minutes.” 

Arian’s hand stills against the horse’s muzzle, he looks at Caleb, before catching his wrist and drawing him against his chest. 

Caleb lets out a startled gasp, his hand coming up instinctively to rest against Arian’s arm. For a moment he can only blink at him, entirely off guard, as Arian’s arm remains around his waist, holding him close enough that Caleb could feel the warmth of him through the fabric of his shirt. 

“Should I be worried about my horse taking away all your attention from me?” 

Caleb smirks. “Oh, are we jealous of the horse now?”

“Perhaps I’ve decided I don't care to share your attention.” 

Caleb’s smile softens. “Then perhaps you should consider that before abandoning me to the company of a particularly temperamental stallion.” 

“An oversight on my part, I guess I’ll have to make amends.” Arian whispers lowering his head, and Caleb tilts his face up to meet him. 

Their first kiss is gentle, almost unhurried. Arian’s hand settles more securely against Caleb’s waist while his other hand comes to rest along his jaw, his thumb brushing the skin just beneath his cheekbone. Caleb melts into the familiar touch with a quiet, satisfied hum, his arms sliding around Arian’s neck. 

God, he missed him. He really missed Arian in the small, ridiculous way that is difficult to explain without sounding hopelessly enamoured. 

Arian pulls back enough to look at him, and Caleb follows without hesitation, catching his mouth again before he could move away. That earns him a faint smile. 

“Someone’s been waiting for me.” 

“Don’t flatter yourself, I’m simply expressing my displeasure at your absence.” 

“That is very convincing.” 

Caleb pinches his side. Arian catches his hand, and brings it to his mouth, as he kisses his knuckles before returning it to his shoulder. The tenderness of the gesture makes something warm flutter beneath Caleb’s ribs. 

Arian kisses him again, and Caleb abandons all thoughts of his surroundings and deepens the kiss, rising onto his toes, pulling Arian closer by the back of his neck, and Arian responds by tightening his arms around Caleb’s waist. He coaxes Caleb’s mouth open, pulling his bottom lip between his, titling his head to deepen the kiss until Caleb forgets his intention to complain about the past two days. 

Nerone makes a disgruntled sound beside them, but Caleb barely registers it. 

When they finally part, it is only because they need to breathe. Caleb remains close, his forehead almost touching Arian’s, his lips warm and slightly swollen. Arian looks equally reluctant to put any distance between them. 

“Are you going to leave again?” Caleb asks softly. 

The question escapes him before he could pass it off as a joke, his fingers curl around the back of Arian’s neck, as he searches his face with an expression that is more hopeful than he intends. 

Arian’s gaze softens. “No.” he brushes his nose against Caleb’s. “I’m yours for the rest of the night.” 

Caleb closes his eyes and hums in satisfaction. “Good.” 

He kisses Arian again, a quick peck that immediately turns into another when Arian follows him. Caleb smiles against his mouth, then pulls back just far enough to speak. 

“Then I suggest we take this upstairs and continue where we left off.”

“I think that’s an excellent idea, Your Highness.”

Caleb is already turning towards the villa, still holding on to Arian, when Arian catches his hand and brings him back for one more kiss. Caleb laughs against his mouth, delighted by the sudden affection, and lets himself be pulled closer. 

They have barely taken a step when someone clears his throat behind them. 

“Boss. My apologies.” 

Arian closes his eyes briefly, and reluctantly pulls away to look at the man standing behind them. Caleb, who still has his arms around Arian’s neck is enjoying himself far too much to pretend otherwise, as he grins over his shoulder at the unfortunate man standing a few feet away. 

“This better be important.” Arian says glaring at the man. 

The man looks entirely unfazed by the interruption, half of the people at Conti household have grown accustomed to finding their boss in various states of affectionate distractions with His Royal Highness, and no one dares to question it. 

“There is someone at the gates, boss. He is asking to see Your Highness.”

The smile fades from Caleb’s face, making way for confusion. “Who is it?” 

“He claims to be an old friend.” 

An old friend? Caleb exchanges a glance with Arian, whose expression has changed almost imperceptibly. The easy warmth from a moment ago has receded behind something more guarded and sharp. 

“Were you expecting anyone?” Arian asks. 

“Of course not. I don’t exactly have a social calendar here, do I?” 

Arian turns towards the man again. “Keep him outside the gates. We’ll meet him there.” 

“Yes, boss.” The man takes his leave as Caleb looks back at Arian, still trying to make sense of the interruption. 

“Who on earth would come here for me?” 

“I suppose we’ll find out.” Arian takes his hand again, fingers firmly closing around as he pulls him along with him. “Stay close to me.” 

Caleb is already sensing the change in Arian, who seems more alert than relaxed as he was a moment ago. As they reach the gates, nothing prepares Caleb for the figure standing by them. 

Dressed in a crisp black and white suit, his posture straight despite the long journey that has brought him here. His expression is soft, almost apprehensive, and when he looks up he sees Caleb approaching, the smile that spreads across his face is so familiar that Caleb feels his heart lurch. 

For a moment he could only stare, and then the man takes a step forward. 

“Your Highness…”

“Paul?” 

The name leaves Caleb’s mouth as little more than a breath, and then he’s moving. He doesn’t remember letting go of Arian’s hand, or crossing the remaining distance. One moment he is standing beside Arian, and the next he is rushing towards the man who has been a part of his life for years.

Paul barely has time to react before Caleb throws himself at him. 

The embrace is so sudden and enthusiastic that Paul staggers half a step backwards, catching Caleb around the shoulders as the prince wraps his arms tightly around him. 

“I can’t believe it’s you,” Caleb breathes. 

“You Highness ... .I can’t…” Paul struggles to draw a breath. “I can’t breathe.” 

“Oh shut up, you can breathe in a minute.” Caleb only hugs him tighter. 

Paul chuckles, and pats him lightly on his shoulder, before they pull away. Caleb laughs, but the sound is caught somewhere in his throat while his eyes began to sting, overwhelmed by the sheer impossibility of seeing someone from home standing in front of him. 

Being surrounded by people who only knew him as a prince for months has made Caleb become accustomed to the unfamiliar rhythm of Sicily, to a house that is slowly becoming home and a man who has somehow become the centre of it. 

But Paul belongs to a life that existed before any of this. A piece of his life that he misses dearly. 

“No way,” he says laughing through the tears gathering in his eyes. “No way, it’s actually you.” 

Paul’s expressions soften as he squeezes his shoulder. “It is, Your Highness. It is good to see you.” he gives a small formal bow, placing a hand on his heart. 

Caleb shakes his head, still unable to quite believe it. “What are you doing here and why didn’t you tell me you were coming?”

“I was hoping to surprise you.” 

“Well you have surprised me quite a lot. I am considering whether to be furious with you for not warning me that you were about to appear out of thin air.”

Paul laughs and Caleb finds himself laughing with him. He is so absorbed in the reunion that he doesn’t notice Arian approaching. 

Only when Paul’s smile falters slightly and his posture straightens, does Caleb look up, still smiling. Arian’s face remains completely composed but distinctly less welcoming than Caleb might have hoped. 

Paul steps back from Caleb, and tilts his head in a small nod. “Mr. Conti.”

“Mr. Walker.” Arian’s voice is polite enough to be impeccable and cold enough to leave no doubt about the distance between them. “To what do I owe the pleasure?”

“I’ve been in Sicily on business, sir. I thought it would be appropriate to pay His Royal Highness a visit while I was here.”

“And what sort of business brings you to Sicily, Mr. Walker?”

Paul pauses for the briefest moment. 

“Paul, how did you know I’d be here?” Caleb asks suddenly. 

“I mean Mr. Conti’s estate isn’t exactly a secret, I thought this would be the most likely place to find you, Your Highness.”

“How fortunate,” Arian remarks. Caleb glances at him. 

He notices that Arian’s expression has not changed much, but there is something deliberate about the way he is looking at Paul, as though he is examining a piece of information that doesn’t quite fit yet. 

“And what business have you been conducting here?” he asks. 

Paul’s smile becomes a little more formal. 

“Princess Matilda asked me to attend to a few matters following the cancellation of Prince Caleb’s diplomatic assignment after His late Majesty’s death.”

Caleb’s head turns sharply. “Is Matilda here?”

“No, she sent me because she couldn’t come herself.”

“Oh.” Caleb’s expression falls a little. “Is she well? How is everyone else? You must tell me everything.”

“In due course,” Arian interrupts. “I’m curious, Mr. Walker. I don’t recall Princess Matilda informing me that she has sent someone to Sicily on her behalf.” 

Paul meets his gaze. “It wasn’t a formal arrangement with you, sir. I was handling matters on behalf of the royal household.”

“And yet you have arrived at my gates without prior notice, claiming to have come on business for a member of the royal family who isn’t here to confirm your account.”

The words are delivered without hostility, which somehow makes them more pointed. Paul’s jaw tightens briefly before he inclines his head. 

“I apologize if my arrival has caused an inconvenience. That wasn’t my intention.”

“Paul, you haven’t caused any inconvenience,” Caleb says at once. He turns towards Arian, smile fading into a frown. 

“Arian.” 

Arian looks at him, as Caleb’s gaze holds a message that is unmistakable. Please stop. 

He understands the look. He has become familiar with Caleb’s expressions that he can often tell what the prince is thinking about. 

But understanding Caleb’s irritation does not mean he intends to abandon his questions. 

Two nights earlier, someone had trespassed into his harbour, an explosion had killed two of his men, and the man they had captured was part of the royal security. Arian had spent the past two days tracing the attack, examining the security footage, and trying to determine how much Andrew already knows about Caleb’s whereabouts. 

Now Caleb’s personal secretary has arrived at his gates, unannounced, claiming to have come to Sicily on unexplained royal business. 

Arian was not born yesterday, while he would not accuse the man without evidence, particularly not in front of Caleb. But neither would he pretend that the timing is unremarkable. Paul could precisely be who he claims to be, an old friend who has travelled to see someone he cares about. 

Or he could be something else entirely. Until Arian knows which, he intends to keep him where he could see him. 

Caleb’s hand comes to rest on Arian’s arm, the touch is gentle, almost pleading. Arian looks down at it, then back at Caleb’s face. 

He can see how much the reunion means to him, the happiness shining through the confusion, the faint embarrassment of having watched Arian interrogate someone he clearly considers a friend. Caleb has been lonely, Arian knows that and he knows how much he misses his sisters, his home and the familiar people who had known him long before Arian entered his life. 

He isn’t going to take this from him. 

He lets out a quiet breath and turns to Paul. “Where are you staying Mr. Walker?”

Paul seems momentarily taken aback. “Oh…at a hotel near the marketplace sir. I intend to remain in Sicily for the entire month, depending on how long the business takes.”

“You are welcome to stay here. I’ll have a guest room prepared for you.” 

Caleb blinks. For a moment he looks from Arian to Paul as though he isn’t entirely sure he has heard correctly. Then his expression transforms, the remaining tension disappearing beneath a wave of relief and affection, his hand on his arm squeezes a little in gratitude. 

“That’s very generous of you, Mr. Conti. Thank you.” Paul inclines his head respectfully. 

Arian returns the gesture with a brief nod. “You’ll be shown to your room once you’ve settled in. If you need anything, speak to the staff.” 

“Of course.” 

Arian doesn’t offer him a warm welcome, or extends anymore conversation. He simply turns and begins to walk back towards the villa, leaving Caleb standing beside Paul with a look of unrestrained delight that Arian feels the faintest pull at the corner of his mouth despite himself. 

For now, he has given Caleb what he wanted, a piece of home. If Paul is innocent, he would have nothing to fear from staying under Arian’s roof, and if he isn’t, Arian would much rather have him where he could keep an eye on him. 

Caleb turns back to Paul and catches him by the arm, as he begins to steer him towards the villa. 

“You have absolutely no idea how many things I need to ask you.” 

“I suspect, alot.” 

“Yes, you are not leaving my side at all for the rest of the evening.” Paul simply laughs and lets himself be pulled.

“You know, I almost forgot how intolerably organized you are.” 

Caleb finds Paul in the guest room, unpacking his belongings. His jacket now hangs neatly in the wardrobe and the leather case that he had brought with him sits open on the writing desk. 

Paul looks up at him and smiles, it fills Caleb with a familiar sense of happiness and he finds himself smiling back as he enters the room. 

“Come and sit with me,” Caleb says, settling into the armchair beside the window.  

Paul takes the seat opposite him. For a few minutes, they speak of familiar things: Eleanor’s latest musical obsession, Cecily’s school going well, Matilda’s relentless schedule. Caleb listens to it all eagerly, laughing at the stories. 

Then Paul’s expression grows more serious, 

“You Highness, there is something else I wanted to speak to you about, specifically about your return to London.”

The smile on Caleb’s face fades slightly. Paul folds his hands together. “Things have been difficult since His Majesty’s death. And King Andrew….he had to take an extraordinary amount of responsibility in a very short time.” 

Caleb looks down at his hands. “He’s worried about you too.” Paul continues. “He wants you home, and beside him supporting the Crown.” 

A familiar unwelcome guilt begins to settle in Caleb’s chest, he has spent months away from London, cut off from his sisters and responsibilities he had once taken for granted. Whatever happened between him and Andrew, whatever doubts he has about his brother’s intentions, he can not pretend that his absence has left the royal family unaffected. 

“I do miss everyone.” he admits quietly. 

“Then perhaps it’s time to come home.”

Caleb is silent for a moment. There is something else he wanted to ask for months, a question that followed him from London to Sicily and refused to leave him alone. 

“Paul, can I ask you something?”

“Of course.”

“About Father,” Caleb hesitates for a minute. “How did he really die?”

Something changes in Paul’s expression, it is fleeting before his face settles again, but Caleb catches it. 

“His Majesty died of a cardiac arrest, he’s been unwell for quite some time” Paul answers. 

Caleb studies him. “That’s what everyone has been told.”

Paul's brow furrows. “Your Highness?”

Caleb looks away. He remembers Arian’s words at the airport in London, the quiet certainty with which he had told him that his father had been murdered. He has never forgotten them, nor has he found the right moment to ask Arian about them again. 

“People talk,” Paul says gently. “There will always be rumors when someone as important as your father dies unexpectedly. But he had been ill. You must not let speculation make this harder for you.”

Caleb manages a small smile. “I miss everyone, and of course I want to come home, I have a duty to the Crown, regardless of what’s happened.” he pauses. “But I can’t simply leave tomorrow. Arian needs to be certain that it’s safe for me to return.”

“Mr. Conti has no obligation to keep you here any longer.”

Caleb’s smile falters. Paul leans forward, a new urgency entering his voice. “You have to understand, Your Highness, Andrew is King now and he is asking you to come home, why should it matter what anyone else thinks.”

“Paul.” Caleb’s voice is quiet, but the warning in it is unmistakable. 

“I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to upset you. It would simply mean a great deal to all of us if you came home.”

Caleb’s expression softens. He reaches across the space between them and rests a hand on Paul’s shoulder. “I will come home eventually, I am not planning to stay in Sicily forever, I just need to make sure that I can return safely.” 

“Just promise me you’ll think about it. I leave in a month and I can make arrangements, and we could travel back together.”

For one dreadful moment Caleb’s heart seems to drop into his stomach. He pictures himself standing at the gates of the villa, walking away from everything and everyone that has slowly become familiar, the life he has started to build…with Arian. 

He can not imagine saying goodbye to him. Not after everything they have been through. But he could hardly tell Paul any of that. 

So he smiles and nods, instead. 

“I’ll think about it.”

Paul seems relieved. “Thank you, Your Highness."

Caleb finds Arain waiting in his room. He is sitting on the edge of the bed, his phone pressed to his ear. When he looks up, whatever he had been about to say on the phone dies on his tongue and he ends the call without another word. 

For reasons Caleb can't quite explain, the sight of him there loosens something tight in his chest. As the tension and the heaviness from his conversation with Paul leaves him, easing him to breathe properly. 

Arian smiles at him softly, as Caleb crosses the room and sits beside him with a tired sigh. 

“Has Paul settled in all right?” Arian asks. 

“Yes, he’s quite comfortable I think.” Caleb glances at him, offering a small smile. “Thank you for letting him stay.”

Arian looks down at the phone in his hand and sighs. “It seemed the best way to keep an eye on him.”

Caleb’s smile disappears. “What is that supposed to mean?”

Arian is quiet for a moment, then he sets his phone aside and turns towards him. 

“Two nights ago, we had an incident at the harbour. Two men trespassed onto Conti territory from a ship. They killed two of my men before we could intercept them, one of them has escaped”

Caleb stares at him, struggling to understand what he was hearing. 

“He was a member of the Royal Guard.”

For a moment, Caleb doesn’t move. Then he is on his feet, staring down at Arian in disbeleif. 

“You knew this for two days and didn’t think it was something I should've been told?”

“I needed to establish what they wanted before I told you anything.”

“You needed to establish….” Caleb stops, a disbelieving laugh escaping him. “Arian, they killed two of your men, someone from the royal palace came here to attack and you thought I didn’t need to know?”

“I was going to tell you eventually, I just needed to be sure first if that was where the attack came from.”

“And now?” 

“Now I know they were sent to bring you back to London. They made it quite clear they were willing to break every bone in your body to accomplish that.”

The color drains from Caleb’s face. 

Arian rises and reaches for Caleb’s hands, taking them gently in his own. “They threatened my men, they threatened you. They made it clear that they have no intention of accepting your continued presence here. And now Paul arrives at my gates without warning, claiming to have come all this way because he’s worried about you.”

He holds Caleb’s gaze. “You can understand why I have to question this timing.”

“Wait a minute.” Caleb shakes his head and pulls away from Arian. “You cannot seriously be suspicious of Paul.”

“I am suspicious of everyone who arrives unexpectedly while someone is actively trying to hunt you down.”

“He is my secretary, Arian. He’s been in my life for years. He’s been with me long before any of this happened, and has never given me a reason to doubt him.”

“You don’t know that.” 

Caleb flinches as though Arian has struck him. 

“Oh, that’s unfair. You’re telling me that the men at the harbour were sent to threaten you because Andrew wants me home, and now you’re suggesting Paul is somehow involved?”

“Andrew wants to keep you where he can control you Caleb, yet you keep assuming that the person sitting on the throne had nothing to fear from you.” 

Arian steps closer, the tenderness in his face from earlier is gone now. “You think because you’ve spent a few months here, the threat is over? That whoever tried to kill you in London has simply forgotten you exist? You know what your brother is capable of” 

Something inside Caleb turns over. He thinks about that night in London, of the violence and confusion and how quickly everything fell apart. He had even considered the possibility that Andrew is involved in all of this, and had forced himself to discard such thoughts. 

Now he is being asked to reconsider that his own brother might have wanted him dead.  

“Why would he do that?” Caleb asks again, looking at him with something more wounded than anger now. “He is already king, I never wanted the crown, I’m the spare. I did everything to avoid being dragged into royal business and now you’re telling me Andrew might have orchestrated an attempt on my life?”

“I’m telling you, you need to stop assuming he wouldn’t.”

“And Paul?” Caleb demands. “Is that what you’re telling me about him too, that he’s part of some elaborate plot to drag me home?” 

“You have reasons to trust Paul, I don’t.”

“That’s convenient, isn’t it? Everytime I ask a question, there is another secret I can’t possibly know about.” 

Arian’s eyes narrow slightly. “Caleb…”

“No, that is your problem Arian. You have never been entirely honest with me. You and Matilda both have made decisions about my life, and decide what I can and cannot know.”

“I am trying to keep you safe.”

“Paul would never hurt me.” Caleb says sharply. 

“I never said he would.” 

“But you don’t trust him, you trust me enough to let me sleep in your bed, kiss me and hold me, but when I tell you that Paul would never betray me, you look at me as though I’m a fool who doesn’t understand the world he’s living in.”

“This isn’t what I think.” Arian says, reaching out for Caleb, but he pulls away. 

“Then what do you think?”

“That you want to believe the best of people who have given you no reason to question them. But sometimes it gets you killed.”

Caleb laughs, thin and bitter. “But do you know what else can hurt me? Finding out that the people I trust most find it convenient to keep things from me in the name of protection.”

His voice falters, as he looks away for a moment blinking hard. 

“And I hate that I have to ask you for the truth, I hate that I have to wonder what else you know that you haven’t told me.”

Arian’s jaw tightens, as his fist clenches on his side. 

“Caleb,” he says quietly. 

He reaches for him again, his hand settling gently around his arm. Caleb doesn’t pull away this time, though he doesn’t move closer either. Arian exhales and lowers his forehead to his. 

“I am sorry.”

The words are so soft, that Caleb almost wishes he doesn’t hear them. They make it harder to remain angry. 

“I’m sorry I didn’t tell you about the attack,” Arian continues. “I should have, you deserved to know.”

Caleb closes his eyes, feels Arian’s soft breath on his cheeks as he takes him into his embrace. 

“I wanted to be certain of what had happened before I put another thing on your shoulders. I was sparing you the uncertainty, I didn’t consider that keeping you in the dark would be worse.”

“I don’t need you to make everything easier for me,” Caleb whispers. “I need you to trust me enough to let me face it with you.”

Arian’s forehead remains against his. “I know,” he says softly. He draws him closer, resting his cheek briefly against Caleb’s hair. Caleb lets himself be held, hides his face in Arian’s shoulder as his fingers tighten around the fabric of his coat. It is the worst part of loving someone, he thinks, how easily a single touch can undo all the anger you had worked so hard to gather. 

For a little while, neither of them speak. 

Then the moment breaks when the vibration from Arian’s phone fills the air. 

Arian lifts his head and glances at the screen. His expression changes as he reads whatever has appeared there, and closes his eyes wearily. 

“I have to take this.” 

Caleb steps back, the warmth between them already beginning to fade. 

When Arian drops the call he turns back to Caleb again and asks. “I’ll come back in an hour.” 

Caleb folds his arms in and shrugs. “Don’t worry, take your time.” 

“Caleb…”

“Just…we’ll talk later okay. You should go.” 

Arian looks at the sudden distance Caleb has put between them, or the careful tone of dismissal in his voice. 

He reaches for him, then pauses, giving Caleb the chance to step away, when he doesn’t, Arian rests a hand against the side of his face, his thumb brushing lightly over his cheek.  

“I’m coming back.” he says quietly. 

Caleb looks down briefly before easing into his touch. “I know.”

That was all, as Caleb watches Arian leave through the door, until his footsteps disappear down the corridor before letting his arms fall to his side. 

The room starts to feel strangely quiet without him again.

The old industrial plant stands beyond the outskirts of town, just a hulking skeleton of concrete and rusted steel that was abandoned years ago. Arian steps out of the car and finds four of his security team standing guard just outside the iron gate. Rainwater has collected in the potholes surrounding the building as Arian walks up to one of his men. 

“He's inside. We found the bastard in the downtown hotel at Palermo, he put us on a bit of a chase.” 

Arian simply nods and enters inside. Rowan is waiting beneath the awning, as Arian falls into step beside him. 

“He’s been talking since we brought him in,” Rowan says. “Mostly nonsense, keep insisting we’re all dead men.” 

“How reassuring.” 

A single work light bulb illuminates the centre of the room, beneath it, a man is tied to a metal chair, his head hanging forward and his clothes filthy with dust. Unlike the man they had captured at the harbour, he is not wearing any Royal Guard’s uniform. He looks rather ordinary who can easily disappear into the crowd. 

The man lifts his head when they approach and begins to laugh menacingly. “So, the Don of Sicily finally comes to see me.” 

Arian stops in front of him, “How did you escape the harbour?”

The man laughs harder. “Do you know what you’ve brought upon yourself, Arian Conti.”

“His Majesty will come for him himself if that’s what it takes. Andrew will tear this island apart to get his brother back. You think you can keep the prince here forever? You are a dead man walking.”

Rowan’s patience snaps, as he steps forward and drives the butt of his gun against the man’s temple. The laughter stops abruptly as the man’s head slumps to one side, and he swears under his breath. 

Arian lifts one hand, stopping him before he could strike again. He has no interest in theatrics. He already heard the threats at the harbour, this man is simply another mouth repeating the same message. 

The prisoner spits blood onto the concrete. “You think I’m afraid of you?”

“No.” Arian glances at the man’s bound hands. “I think you’re hoping I’ll be distracted by your courage long enough for you to forget how little of it you actually possess.”

“He’s coming for the prince, Conti. And when he does, there won’t be anything you can do about it.”

Arian looks at him for another second, and his jaw tightens. He remembers Caleb standing in his bedroom, pleading for honesty and how he hadn’t been able to answer without exposing how much of Caleb’s world had been built on lies. 

He never wants to keep things from Caleb but the thought of an active threat suddenly surfacing has put his life in danger again. These men are multiplying, and god only knows what else Andrew has set in motion. 

Sicily is not safe anymore, and keeping Caleb safe is becoming difficult by the hour. 

Arian draws his gun out, and the prisoner’s smile falters. 

“Wait…”

The shot cracks through the building, as the man slumps against the chair, his head falling forward. The sound reverberates off the high concrete walls before dissolving into silence as blood drips down the man’s forehead. 

Rowan looks at him with raised eyebrows. “That was one way to end the conversation.” 

“He had nothing useful to say.”

Arian lowers his gun and has barely taken a step towards the exit when a shout rings out from the far side of the building. 

“Boss!” 

The man posted outside doesn’t get the chance to finish speaking when the first gunshot echoes through the empty lot. 

Rowan’s head snaps towards the entrance as the second shot follows. Then comes the unmistakable crash of the doors being forced open. Six men pour into the building, all holding machine guns, already firing. 

“DOWN!”

Arian moves before the word has fully escaped Rowan’s mouth. 

He throws himself behind a concrete pillar as bullets tear through the work light above the chair. Glass bursts and soon the room is plunged into darkness, save for the thin grey light filtering through the broken windows and the intermittent flashes of gunfire. 

One of Arian’s men goes down almost immediately. 

Another drags him towards cover while shouting for the others to spread out. Arian presses his back against the pillar, gun raised and takes a single measured breath. He takes note of all six men, armed and coordinated. He leans out just far enough to see the nearest assailant advancing across the open floor. The man has his weapon trained towards Rowan, attention fixed on the wrong target. 

Arian fires once. The bullet strikes between the man’s eyes and he drops before he could turn, his weapon clattering against the concrete. 

Arian withdraws behind the pillar as another volley tears through the wall where his head had been. Across the room, Rowan returns fire as one of the attackers stumbles backwards and disappears behind a rusted machine, but Arian can’t tell whether the shot has killed him. 

Arian quickly moves close to the staircase behind the pillar and takes two steps at a time, reaching the upper walkway overlooking most of the main floor, giving him the better view of the men below. 

He recognizes one of them. The realization is immediate and unwelcomed, its one of the palace Royal Guard, one Arian had encountered and worked with on more than an occasion during his time in London. 

So Andrew has stopped pretending. 

Arian reaches the upper walkway and drops behind a rusted support beam just as a bullet strikes the railing beside him. 

There are two men advancing along the ground floor, another has moved towards the staircase. Their position obscured by the smoke beginning to gather beneath the ceiling. 

How many more had Andrew sent? The sudden dread arrives uninvited at the thought, leaving his blood running cold. 

Caleb. 

The villa is secure. He knows that his men are stationed through the grounds, the perimeter is monitored, and the security systems are among the best money could buy. Caleb is safer there than he would be anywhere else on the island. 

Nevertheless, he needs to get back. 

Arian feels a figure emerge from behind the machinery and he turns and fires. 

The man ducks, returning fire as he runs towards the staircase. Arian feels the vibration of the bullet striking the beam beside his shoulder. He shifts, waits for the man to expose himself again, and fires at his legs.

The attacker collapses with a cry, his weapon skidding across the floor. Arian keeps his gun trained on him as he nears him, the man is trying to reach for his weapon. Arian fires once, the man stops moving. 

Three down, three remaining.

Below, Rowan has taken up a position behind a heavy piece of machinery, directing the surviving men into cover and firing wherever one of the attackers exposed himself. 

Just as Arian reaches the bottom of the staircase, a shadow moves at the edge of his vision, he turns too late. The blow strikes his gun from his hand with enough force to send the weapon flying across the concrete. Before he could recover, a boot catches him across the face. 

His head snaps sideways. He stumbles and hits the ground hard and tastes blood. 

The man comes at him immediately, but Arian rolls away at times, as a fist strikes the floor where his head had been. He catches the attacker’s arm and uses his momentum to pull him off balance, and drives his shoulder into the man’s chest. They crash into the side of the machine. 

The attacker is strong and considerably heavier than Arian, and he uses that advantage to slam him against the metal again. Arian feels the impact along his ribs, but he is quick to recover and answers with an elbow to the man’s jaw. 

The man staggers a bit, but doesn’t fall. 

Arian strikes him again, catches the front of his jacket, and drives him down onto the concrete. He lands on top of him, pining the man’s gun hand beneath his knee before the weapon could be used. 

The attacker twists violently, trying to wrench his hand free. Arian drives his fist into the man’s face, once, twice. 

The man bucks beneath him, then manages to bring his free hand around and punch Arian below his jaw. Arian’s vision flashes white. For one brief, infuriating second, the man gains enough leverage to drag the gun hand towards himself. 

Arian catches his wrist. 

“You’re making this unnecessarily difficult,” he growls, forcing the man’s arm away from the weapon and twists it until the joint gives. 

The scream rings through the building.

Arian rises, dragging the man with him just long enough to wrench his arm behind his back. He drives him face-first against the machine, disarms him, and sends him sprawling to the floor. The man scrambles towards Arian’s fallen gun, but Arian is quick to seize him from behind. In one swift motion, he grabs the man’s head and twists. 

There is a sharp crack, and the body goes limp. 

For a moment, Arian remains standing over him, breathing hard, blood running from the cut at his cheek, dust clinging to his black shirt and trousers. His knuckles throb and somewhere beneath his ribs, a familiar ache has begun to spread. 

He bends down to retrieve his gun, and checks the bullets. 

He looks towards the main floor, Rowan has taken down another man. 

“Rowan!” 

“Busy!” 

Arian almost smiles and then starts towards the staircase keeping his weapon trained on the last visible attacker. His mind is already calculating how quickly he could get to the car, how many calls he needs to make, how to secure the villa before anything else happens. 

He needs to go back to Caleb, he needs to see him. 

The thought has barely formed when he reaches the bottom steps and glances across the room. Five bodies lay scattered across the concrete. 

Five. 

Arian stops. 

There were six men at the entrance. For one terrible second the room goes silent around him. And then he hears the movement behind him. He turns as the sixth man is standing barely a few feet away, gun already raised. 

Arian takes a sharp breath, and he barely has time to move when the gunshot cracks through the building. 

Pain explodes through his side. He staggers back, as the bullet strikes just beneath his ribs, the impact driving the breath from his lungs. For a moment he can’t understand what has happened, he looks down almost stupidly as though the dark stain spreading across his black shirt belongs to someone else. 

Vicious, blinding pain catches up with him as his knees buckle, and he falls to the floor hard, catching himself on one arm. His other hand clamps over the wound, blood seeps between his fingers, hot against his skin. 

“ARIAN!”

Rowan’s shout tears through the building. Arian tries to get up but his body refuses to cooperate. 

He drags himself backwards, teeth clenched against the pain, trying to put distance between himself and the gun still pointed at his chest. The movement sends a fresh wave of agony through his side, his breath catches low, as an involuntary sound escapes him. 

No.

Not here. 

Caleb…

I’m coming back. 

I know. 

Arian’s palm is slick with blood, he can hear Rowan shouting orders, as well as the pounding of boots against the concrete but his attention remains fixed on the man standing over him, looking down at him.

Arian’s vision has already gone blurred when the man lifts his weapon and pulls the trigger.
